## Page 9221

The eighth hadith:

The imam, scholar, and ascetic Kamāl al-Dīn Abū Zakariyyā Yahyā ibn Abī Mansūr ibn Abī al-Fath ibn Rāfi ibn Alī al-Harrānī, Ibn al-Sayrafī, informed us through a reading to him in Shawwāl 668 AH: Abū al-Abbās Ahmad ibn Yahyā ibn Barakah, Ibn al-Dībqī, informed us through a reading to him which I heard: Abū Mansūr Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wāhid ibn al-Hasan al-Qazzāz informed us through a reading to him on 21 Jumādā al-Ūlā 534 AH: Abū Jafar Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Umar ibn al-Muslim al-Muaddil informed us by dictation from his own lips, assisted in the dictation by our shaykh Abū Bakr al-Khatīb, in Safar 463 AH: Abū al-Fadl Ubayd Allāh ibn Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad al-Zuhrī informed us: Abū Bakr Jafar ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan ibn al-Mustafād al-Firyābī informed us: Qutaybah ibn Saīd narrated to us: Ismāīl ibn Jafar narrated to us, from Abū Suhayl Nāfi ibn Mālik ibn Abī Āmir, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah, from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he makes a promise, he breaks it; and when he is entrusted with something, he betrays that trust.”

---

## Page 9222

The ninth hadith:

The shaykh, jurist, imam, and accomplished scholar Jamāl al-Dīn Abū al-Faraj Abd al-Rahmān ibn Sulaymān ibn Saīd ibn Sulaymān al-Baghdādī informed us through a reading to him which I heard in 668 AH: Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zabad al-Kindī informed us through a reading to him: Abū Abd Allāh al-Husayn ibn Alī ibn Ahmad ibn al-Muqrī informed us: Abū al-Husayn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn al-Naqqūr informed us: Abū Tāhir Muhammad ibn Abd al-Rahmān ibn al-Abbās al-Mukhlis informed us in 390 AH: Yahyā narrated to us: Yūnus narrated to us: Abū al-Ahwas narrated to us, from Ashath ibn Abī al-Shathā, from Muhammad ibn Umayr, from Abū Hurayrah, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prohibited two kinds of sale and two ways of wearing garments: a man wearing a single garment, wrapping himself in it and throwing one of its sides over his shoulder; sitting with his knees drawn up and held against his body by a single garment; and saying, ‘Throw your garment to me, and I will throw mine to you,’ without either examining the garments.”

He was born in Harrān in 585 AH and died in Damascus in Shabān 670 AH.

---

## Page 9223

The tenth hadith:

Sharaf al-Dīn Abū Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Abd al-Munim ibn Umar ibn Abd Allāh ibn Ghadīr ibn al-Qawwās al-Tāī informed us through a reading to him which I heard in 675 AH, and Abū al-Hasan ibn al-Bukhārī also informed us. They both said:

Abū al-Abbās al-Khadir ibn Kāmil ibn Sālim al-Sarūjī informed us through a reading to him: Abū Abd Allāh al-Husayn ibn Alī ibn Ahmad al-Muqrī informed us.

Al-Fakhr al-Bukhārī also said:

Abū al-Yaman al-Kindī informed us: Abū al-Qāsim Ismāīl ibn Ahmad ibn Umar al-Samarqandī informed us. They both said: Abū al-Husayn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Abd Allāh ibn al-Naqqūr informed us: Abū al-Husayn Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh ibn al-Husayn ibn Abd Allāh ibn Hārūn, Ibn Akhī Mīmīyī al-Daqqāq, informed us: Abd Allāh narrated to us: Dāwūd narrated to us: al-Walīd ibn Muslim narrated to us, from Abū Ghassān Muhammad ibn Mutarrif, from Zayd ibn Aslam, from Alī ibn al-Husayn, from Saīd ibn Marjānah, from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“Whoever frees a slave, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will free, for each of the slave’s limbs, a corresponding limb of his

---

## Page 9224

from the Fire, even his private parts in return for the slave’s private parts.”

Al-Bukhārī narrated it from Muhammad ibn Abd al-Rahīm, from Dāwūd ibn Rashīd, while Muslim narrated it directly from Dāwūd himself. Al-Tirmidhī narrated it from Qutaybah, from al-Layth, from Ibn al-Hād, from Umar ibn Alī ibn al-Husayn, from Saīd ibn Marjānah. He was born in 602 AH and died in Rabī al-Ākhir, 682 AH.

The eleventh hadith:

The righteous shaykhs and hadith transmitters—Abū Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Badr ibn Muhammad ibn Yaīsh al-Jazarī, Abū al-Abbās Ahmad ibn Shaybān, Abū al-Fadl Ismāīl ibn Abī Abd Allāh ibn al-Asqalānī, and Zaynab bint Ahmad ibn Kāmil—informed us through a reading to them which I heard in Shabān, 675 AH, at Qāsiyūn. They said:

Abū Hafs Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh al-Baghdādī informed us through a reading to him which we heard: Abū al-Qāsim Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad ibn Abd al-Qādir ibn Yūsuf, Abū Mansūr Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wāhid al-Qazzāz, and Abū al-Fath Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Muhammad al-Baydāwī informed us through a reading to them which I heard. They said:

Abū Jafar Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn al-Muslim al-Muaddil informed us: Abū Tāhir Muhammad ibn

---

## Page 9225

Abd al-Rahmān ibn al-Abbās al-Mukhlis informed us: Abū al-Qāsim Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Azīz al-Baghawī informed us: Abd Allāh ibn Mutī related to me: Ismāīl ibn Jafar narrated to us.

Al-Baghawī said: Sālih ibn Mālik also related to me: Abd al-Azīz ibn Abd Allāh narrated to us. Al-Baghawī said: My grandfather also related to me: Yazīd ibn Hārūn narrated to us. All of them narrated from Humayd, from Anas, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I entered Paradise and came upon a palace of gold. I asked, ‘Whose palace is this?’ They replied, ‘It belongs to a young man from Quraysh.’ I thought that I was that man, so I asked, ‘Who is he?’ They replied, ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb.’”

The wording is Ibn Mutī’s. He died in Shabān, 675 AH.

The twelfth hadith:

The jurist and imam, the learned scholar who put his knowledge into practice, Zayn al-Dīn Abū Ishāq Ibrāhīm ibn Ahmad ibn Abī al-Faraj ibn Abī Tāhir ibn Muhammad ibn Nasr, known as Ibn al-Sadīd, informed us

---

## Page 9226

al-Ansārī al-Hanafī, through a reading to him in Rajab, 675 AH: Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd al-Kindī informed us through a reading to him. Zaynab bint Makkī also informed us, saying: Abū Hafs ibn Tabarzadh informed us.

They both said: The judge Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abd al-Bāqī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Ansārī informed us: Abū al-Hasan Alī ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Īsā al-Bāqilānī informed us: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Jafar ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī'ī narrated to us: Muhammad ibn Mūsā al-Qurashī narrated to us: Awn ibn Imāra narrated to us: Humayd al-Tawīl narrated to us, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“One who is fasting has the choice until midday.”

He died in Jumādā al-Ūlā, 677 AH, aged seventy-three.

The thirteenth hadith:

The shaykh and imam, the Quran reciter and distinguished notable, Kamāl al-Dīn Abū Ishāq Ibrāhīm ibn Ahmad ibn Ismāīl ibn Fāris al-Tamīmī al-Sadī, informed us through a reading to him, while I listened, in Ramadan, 674 AH: Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd informed us

---

## Page 9227

al-Kindī: The judge Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abd al-Bāqī al-Ansārī informed us: Abū al-Husayn Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Hasanūn al-Narsī informed us in 455 AH: Abū Tāhir Muhammad ibn Abd al-Rahmān al-Mukhlis informed us: Abū al-Qāsim Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad al-Baghawī narrated to us: Shurayh ibn Yūnus, Muhammad ibn Yazīd al-Ādamī, Ibn al-Bazzār, and Hārūn ibn Abd Allāh narrated to us. They said:

Ma'n narrated to us, from Mu'āwiya ibn Sālih, from Buhayr ibn Sa'd, from Khālid ibn Ma'dān, from Uqba ibn Āmir al-Juhanī, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“One who recites the Quran privately is like one who gives charity secretly, and one who recites the Quran openly is like one who gives charity openly.”

This was also related to us through a chain one degree higher, with a corresponding transmission from Ahmad ibn Abd al-Dā'im: Ibn Kulayb informed us: Ibn Bayān informed us: Ibn Makhlad narrated to us: al-Saffār informed us: Ibn Arafa narrated to us: Ismā'īl ibn Ayyāsh narrated to us, from Buhayr (and he related the hadith).

He was born in 596 AH and died in Safar, 676 AH.

The fourteenth hadith:

The imam and hadith transmitter, Zayn al-Dīn Abū al-Abbās Ahmad ibn Abī al-Khayr Salāma ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Salāma ibn al-Haddād al-Dimashqī, informed us through my reading to him, while I

---

## Page 9228

listened, in Rabī' al-Awwal, 675 AH. I said to him:

Abū Sa'īd Khalīl ibn Abī al-Rajā' ibn Abī al-Fath al-Rārānī informed you by authorization.

It was also read to my father, while I listened, in Harrān in 666 AH: Yūsuf ibn Khalīl informed you: al-Rārānī informed us: Abū Alī al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn al-Hasan al-Haddād informed us: the hadith master Abū Nu'aym Ahmad ibn Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad ibn Ishāq informed us: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Yūsuf ibn Khallād informed us: al-Hārith ibn Abī Usāma narrated to us: Abd Allāh ibn Bakr narrated to us: Humayd narrated to us, from Anas, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw a rope stretched between two pillars of the mosque. He asked, ‘What is this rope?’

“They replied, ‘O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so prays as long as she remains alert; when drowsiness overcomes her, she holds on to it.’

“He said, ‘Let her pray as long as she remains alert; when drowsiness overcomes her, let her sleep.’”

He was born in Rabī' al-Awwal, 609 AH, and died on the day of Āshūrā', 678 AH.

The fifteenth hadith:

The upright hadith transmitter Amīn al-Dīn Abū Muhammad al-Qāsim ibn Abī Bakr ibn Qāsim ibn Ghunayma al-Irbilī informed us, as did Abū Bakr ibn Umar ibn Yūnus al-Mizzī al-Hanafī

---

## Page 9229

and Abū Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Sulaymān al-Āmirī, as it was read to them while I listened in 677 AH.

The first said:

Abū al-Hasan al-Mu’ayyad informed us, from Muhammad ibn al-Fadl ibn Ahmad al-Furāwī.

The other two said:

Abū al-Qāsim Abd al-Samad ibn al-Harastānī informed us, as it was read to him: al-Furāwī informed us by authorization: Abū al-Husayn Abd al-Ghāfir ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Ghāfir al-Fārisī informed us: Abū Ahmad Muhammad ibn Īsā ibn Amrawayh al-Julūdī informed us: Abū Ishāq Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn Sufyān informed us: Muslim ibn al-Hajjāj al-Qushayrī narrated to us: Khalaf ibn Hishām, Abū al-Rabī‘ al-Zahrānī, and Qutayba ibn Sa‘īd narrated to us, all from Hammād.

Khalaf said:

Hammād ibn Zayd narrated to us, from Muhammad ibn Ziyād: Abū Hurayra narrated to us, saying: Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Does the one who raises his head before the imam not fear that Allah may turn his head into a donkey’s head?”

Al-Irbilī was born in Irbil in 595 AH or earlier and died in Jumādā al-Ūlā, 680 AH. Al-Mizzī was born in 593 AH and died in Sha‘bān, 680 AH.

---

## Page 9230

Hadith 16:

The shaykh, imam, scholar, and chief judge Shams al-Dīn Abū Muhammad Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Atā ibn Hasan al-Hanafī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 667 AH, together with Abū al-Abbās ibn Allān and Abū al-Abbās ibn Shaybān. They said:

Abū Alī Hanbal ibn Abd Allāh ibn al-Faraj al-Rusāfī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū al-Qāsim Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wāhid ibn al-Husayn al-Shaybānī informed us: Abū Alī al-Hasan ibn Alī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Madhhab al-Tamīmī informed us: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Ja‘far ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī‘ī informed us: Abū Abd al-Rahmān Abd Allāh, son of Imam Abū Abd Allāh Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Hanbal al-Shaybānī—may Allah be pleased with him—narrated to us: My father, Ahmad ibn Muhammad, narrated to me: Sufyān narrated to us, from Abd Allāh ibn Dīnār: I heard Umar say: The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever keeps a dog—other than a dog for livestock or hunting—will have his reward reduced by two qirāts each day.”

---

## Page 9231

He was born in 595 AH and died in Jumādā al-Ūlā, 673 AH.

Hadith 17:

The shaykh, imam, scholar, eminent authority, ascetic, and chief judge Shams al-Dīn Abū Muhammad Abd al-Rahmān ibn Abī Umar Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Qudāmah al-Maqdisī al-Hanbalī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Sha‘bān, 667 AH, at Qāsiyūn, together with Ibn Shaybān, Ibn al-Asqalānī, and Ibn al-Hamawī. They said:

Abū Hafs Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh informed us: Abū al-Qāsim Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wāhid ibn al-Husayn informed us: Abū Tālib Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ghaylān al-Bazzāz informed us: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh ibn Ibrāhīm al-Shāfi‘ī informed us: Muhammad ibn Salamah al-Wāsitī narrated to us: Yazīd ibn Hārūn narrated to us: Sulaymān al-Taymī narrated to us, from Abū Uthmān al-Nahdī, from Abū Mūsā al-Ash‘arī—may Allah be pleased with him—who said:

“We were with the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and the people were climbing a steep ascent or a mountain pass. Whenever a man climbed, he would say, ‘There is no god but Allah, and Allah is the Greatest’—the narrator said: I think he said, ‘at the top of his voice’—while the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—was riding his mule, guiding it along the mountain. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said, ‘O Abū Mūsā,

---

## Page 9232

you are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent.’ Then he said, ‘O Abd Allāh ibn Qays—or O Abū Mūsā—shall I direct you to a phrase from the treasures of Paradise?’ I said, ‘Yes, O Messenger of Allah.’ He said, ‘Say: There is no power and no strength except through Allah.’”

He was born in 597 AH and died in 682 AH.

Hadith 18:

The upright hadith transmitter of distinguished lineage, Majd al-Dīn Abū Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Ismā‘īl ibn Uthmān ibn al-Muzaffar ibn Hibat Allāh ibn Asākir al-Dimashqī, informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Sha‘bān, 667 AH:

The hadith master Abū Muhammad al-Qāsim ibn Alī ibn al-Hasan ibn Hibat Allāh ibn Asākir informed us, as it was read to him: Abū al-Durr Yāqūt ibn Abd Allāh al-Rūmī, the merchant and client of Ibn al-Bukhārī, informed us, as it was read to him.

Zaynab bint Makkī and Ismā‘īl ibn al-Asqalānī also informed us, saying:

Ibn Tabarzadh informed us: Judge Abū Bakr al-Ansārī, Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn al-Ashqar al-Dallāl, Abū Ghālib Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Quraysh, and Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Dahrūj informed us.

They all said:

Abū Muhammad Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh ibn

---

## Page 9233

Hazār Mard al-Sarīfīnī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Tāhir Muhammad ibn Abd al-Rahmān ibn al-Abbās al-Mukhlis narrated to us by dictation in Sha‘bān, 393 AH: Abū al-Qāsim Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn al-Baghawī narrated to us: Shaybān ibn Farrūkh narrated to us: Mubārak ibn Fadāla narrated to us: al-Hasan narrated to us, from Anas, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, used to deliver the Friday sermon beside a wooden post, resting his back against it. When the people grew more numerous, he said, ‘Build me a pulpit with two steps.’ When he stood on the pulpit to deliver the sermon, the wooden post cried out in longing for the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him.

Anas said: ‘I was in the mosque and heard the wooden post crying like one distraught with longing. It continued crying until he came down to it and embraced it; then it fell silent.’

Whenever al-Hasan related this hadith, he would weep and then say: ‘O servants of Allah! This wooden post cries out in longing for the Messenger of Allah because of his standing with Allah, Mighty and Majestic. You have even greater reason to yearn to meet him.’”

He was born in 587 AH and died in Dhū al-Qa‘da, 699 AH.

Hadith 19:

The shaykh, imam and eminent dignitary, Shams al-Dīn Abū al-Ghanā’im al-Muslim ibn Muhammad ibn al-Muslim ibn Allān al-Qaysī, informed us, as it was read to him while I listened, in the year

---

## Page 9234

680 AH, as did Abū al-Hasan ibn al-Bukhārī. Both said:

Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd al-Kindī informed us, as it was read to him: The judge Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abd al-Bāqī ibn Muhammad al-Ansārī informed us: Abū Muhammad al-Hasan ibn Alī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan al-Jawharī narrated to us by dictation: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Ja‘far ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī‘ī informed us: Bishr ibn Mūsā narrated to us: Abū Nu‘aym narrated to us: al-A‘mash narrated to us, from Abū Sālih, from Abū Hurayra, who said: The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘Fasting is for Me, and I shall reward it. He gives up his desire, food and drink for My sake. Fasting is a shield, and the fasting person has two joys: joy when he breaks his fast, and joy when he meets Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Indeed, the odor of the fasting person’s mouth is more pleasant to Allah than the scent of musk.’”

He was born in 594 AH and died on the sixth of Dhū al-Hijja, 680 AH.

Hadith 20:

The dignitary, Imād al-Dīn Abū Muhammad Abd al-Rahmān ibn Abī al-Su‘r ibn al-Sayyid ibn al-Sāni‘ al-Ansārī, informed us, as it was read to him while I listened, in the year 676 AH

---

## Page 9235

and Abū al-Izz Yūsuf ibn Ya‘qūb ibn al-Mujāwir and al-Muslim ibn Allān. They said:

Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd al-Kindī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Mansūr Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad ibn Zurayq al-Qazzāz al-Shaybānī informed us, as it was read to him: The hadith master Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Alī ibn Thābit al-Khatīb al-Baghdādī informed us: Abū Umar Abd al-Wāhid ibn Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh ibn Mahdī informed us: The judge Abū Abd Allāh al-Husayn ibn Ismā‘īl al-Mahāmilī narrated to us: Abū Mūsā Muhammad ibn al-Muthannā narrated to us: Ibn Uyayna narrated to us, from Hishām ibn Urwa, from his father, from Ā’isha, may Allah be pleased with her:

“When the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, came to Mecca, he entered it from its upper end and departed from its lower end.”

Al-Bukhārī, Muslim, Abū Dāwūd, al-Tirmidhī and al-Nasā’ī narrated it from Abū Mūsā. He died in Ramadan, 679 AH.

Hadith 21:

Abū Ishāq Ibrāhīm ibn Ismā‘īl ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Yahyā ibn Ulwī ibn al-Husayn al-Darjī al-Qurashī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened, in Rajab, 680 AH

---

## Page 9236

: Abū Ja‘far Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Nasr ibn Abī al-Fath al-Saydalānī informed us by authorization to transmit: Abū Alī al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn al-Hasan al-Haddād informed us: The hadith master Abū Nu‘aym Ahmad ibn Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad ibn Ishāq informed us: Abū Muhammad Abd Allāh ibn Ja‘far ibn Ahmad ibn Fāris informed us, saying: I heard Sufyān ibn Uyayna say:

Āsim narrated to us, from Zirr, who said:

“I came to Safwān ibn Assāl al-Murādī, and he asked me, ‘What brings you here?’

“I replied, ‘I have come to seek knowledge.’

“He said, ‘The angels lower their wings for the seeker of knowledge, pleased with what he seeks.’

“I said, ‘I have felt some misgiving in my heart—or my breast—about wiping over leather socks after defecating or urinating. Have you heard anything about this from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him?’

“He replied, ‘Yes. When we were on a journey—or travelling—he would instruct us not to remove our leather socks for three days and nights, except in a state of janābah (major ritual impurity), but not because of defecation, urination or sleep.’

“I asked, ‘Did you hear him speak of guidance?’

“He replied, ‘Yes. While we were travelling with him, a Bedouin called out to him in a loud voice, saying, “O Muhammad!” He answered him in a similar tone, “Here I am!” The man asked, “What do you say about a man who loves some people but has not joined them?”

“‘He replied, “A person will be with those he loves.”

“‘He then went on to tell us that in the west there is a gate which Allah, Mighty and Majestic, opens for repentance. Its breadth is a journey of forty years, and it will not be closed until the sun rises from that direction. This is what Allah says:

“On the Day some of your Lord’s signs come, no soul will benefit from its faith.”’” … the rest of the verse.

He was born in 599 AH and died in Safar, 671 AH.

---

## Page 9237

Hadith 22:

Najīb al-Dīn Abū al-Murhaf al-Miqdād ibn Abī al-Qāsim Hibat Allāh ibn al-Miqdād ibn Alī al-Qaysī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened: Abū Muhammad Abd al-Azīz ibn Mahmūd ibn al-Mubārak ibn al-Akhdar informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abd al-Bāqī al-Ansārī informed us: Abū Ishāq al-Barmakī informed us: Abū Muhammad ibn Māsī informed us: Abū Muslim al-Kajjī narrated to us: Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh al-Ansārī narrated to us: Sulaymān al-Taymī narrated to me, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Muslims must not shun one another for more than three days”—or he said, “three nights.”

Hadith 23:

Imam Abū Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Āmir ibn Abī Bakr al-Ghasūlī informed us, through my reading to him in 682 AH: Abū al-Barakāt Dāwūd ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Mulā‘ib informed us, as it was read to him: Abū al-Fadl Muhammad ibn Umar ibn Yūsuf informed us

---

## Page 9238

al-Urmawī, as it was read to him: Abū al-Ghanā’im Abd al-Samad ibn Alī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Ma’mūn informed us: Abū al-Hasan Alī ibn Umar ibn Ahmad ibn Mahdī al-Dāraqutnī informed us: Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abd al-Azīz al-Baghawī narrated to us: Sālih ibn Hātim ibn Wardān narrated to us: al-Mu‘tamir ibn Sulaymān narrated to us: Abd al-Razzāq narrated to me, from Ma‘mar, from al-Zuhrī, from {Āmir ibn Sa‘d, from his father, who said:

I said, “O Messenger of Allah, you gave to so-and-so and so-and-so, but withheld from so-and-so, although he is a believer.”

He replied, “Or a Muslim.”}

He died in Jumādā al-Ākhirah in 684 AH, when he was nearly eighty.

Hadith 24:

Shaykh Fakhr al-Dīn Abū al-Hasan Alī ibn Ahmad ibn Abd al-Wāhid ibn Ahmad ibn Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ismā‘īl ibn Mansūr ibn al-Bukhārī al-Maqdisī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 681 AH, as did Shaykh Shams al-Dīn Abd al-Rahmān ibn Abī Umar in 667 AH: Abū al-Mahāsin Muhammad ibn Kāmil ibn Ahmad al-Tanūkhī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Muhammad Tāhir ibn Sahl ibn Bishr al-Isfarāyīnī informed us: Abū al-Qāsim al-Husayn ibn al-Hasan ibn Muhammad ibn Ibrāhīm al-Hinnā’ī informed us

---

## Page 9239

that Abū al-Hasan Abd al-Wahhāb ibn al-Walīd ibn Mūsā ibn Rāshid ibn Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Abd Allāh al-Kilābī narrated to us in his own words: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Khuraym ibn Marwān al-Uqaylī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened: Abū al-Walīd Hishām ibn Ammār ibn Nusayr ibn Maysarah al-Sulamī informed us: Mālik ibn Anas narrated to us: Ishāq ibn Abd Allāh ibn Abī Talhah narrated to us, from Anas ibn Mālik, that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

{A good dream seen by a righteous man is one of forty-six parts of prophethood.}

Al-Bukhārī narrated it from al-Qa‘nabī, from Mālik. He was born at the end of 595 AH and died in Rabī‘ al-Ākhir in 690 AH.

Hadith 25:

Abū al-Abbās Ahmad ibn Shaybān ibn Taghlib ibn Haydarah al-Shaybānī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 684 AH: Abū Hafs Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh al-Baghdādī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Ghālib Ahmad ibn al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn Abd Allāh ibn al-Bannā’ informed us, as it was read to him while we listened: Abū Muhammad al-Hasan ibn Alī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan ibn Abd Allāh al-Jawharī informed us, as it was read to him

---

## Page 9240

in Ramadan of 452 AH: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Ja‘far ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī‘ī informed us, as it was read to him while I was present and listening: Abū Alī Bishr ibn Mūsā ibn Sālih al-Asadī narrated to us: Abū Nu‘aym narrated to us: al-A‘mash narrated to us, from Shaqīq ibn Salamah, who said:

Abd Allāh, may Allah be pleased with him, said: “When we prayed behind the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, we would say: ‘Peace be upon Allah rather than upon Allah’s servants. Peace be upon Gabriel and Michael. Peace be upon so-and-so and so-and-so.’

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, turned towards us and said: ‘Allah Himself is the Source of Peace. When any of you prays, let him say: All greetings, prayers, and good things belong to Allah. Peace be upon you, O Prophet, and Allah’s mercy and blessings. Peace be upon us and upon Allah’s righteous servants. I bear witness that none has the right to be worshipped except Allah, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger.’”

Al-Bukhārī related it, and Muslim related it from Ibn al-Muthannā, from Ghundar, from Shu‘bah, from Mansūr—both through Shaqīq. He was born in 599 AH and died in Safar of 685 AH.

---

## Page 9241

Hadith Twenty-Six:

Abū Yahyā Ismā‘īl ibn Abī ‘Abd Allāh ibn Hammād ibn ‘Abd al-Karīm al-‘Asqalānī informed us, through my reading to him in 681 AH, as did Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān, al-Jammāl Ahmad ibn Abī Bakr al-Hamawī, Abū al-Hasan ibn al-Bukhārī, and ‘Alī ibn Mahmūd ibn Shihāb. They said:

Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh al-Baghdādī informed us, as it was read to him: Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn al-Husayn al-Shaybānī informed us: Abū Tālib Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ghaylān al-Bazzār informed us: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn Ibrāhīm al-Shāfi‘ī informed us: Abū al-Hasan ‘Alī ibn al-Hasan ibn ‘Abdawayh al-Jarrār informed us: ‘Abd Allāh ibn Bakr al-Sahmī narrated to us: Humayd narrated to us, from Anas, who said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was on a road with some of his Companions when a woman approached him and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, I have something to ask of you.’ He said, ‘O mother of so-and-so, sit down at the nearest spot in the lanes so that I may sit with you.’ She did so, and he sat with her until she had explained what she needed.”

Ahmad related it from ‘Abd Allāh ibn Bakr.

---

## Page 9242

Ibn al-‘Asqalānī heard hadith in his fourth year, in 599 AH, and died in Ramadan 682 AH. Ibn Shihāb was born in 595 AH and died in Ramadan 680 AH.

Hadith Twenty-Seven:

The venerable and righteous shaykh Kamāl al-Dīn Abū Muhammad ‘Abd al-Rahīm ibn ‘Abd al-Malik ibn Yūsuf ibn Qudāmah al-Maqdisī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Safar 680 AH, as did Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān:

Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh al-Baghdādī informed us, as it was read to him: Judge Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Bāqī ibn Muhammad al-Bazzār and Abū al-Mawāhib Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Malik ibn Mulūk al-Warrāq informed us. They said:

Judge Abū al-Tayyib Tāhir ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Tabarī informed us: Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn al-Ghitrīf informed us: Abū Khalīfah narrated to us: Muslim ibn Ibrāhīm narrated to us, from Hishām and Shu‘bah, from Qatādah, from Sa‘īd ibn al-Musayyib, from Ibn ‘Abbās, who said: Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“One who takes back his gift is like one who returns to his vomit.”

Related by both al-Bukhārī and Muslim. He was born around 598 AH and died in Jumādā al-Ūlā 680 AH.

---

## Page 9243

Hadith Twenty-Eight:

The trustworthy shaykh Zayn al-Dīn Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Abī Tāhir Ismā‘īl ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Abd al-Muhsin al-Anmātī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Rajab 668 AH, as did Abū Hāmid ibn al-Sābūnī and al-Rashīd Muhammad ibn Muhammad al-‘Āmirī. They said:

Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd al-Samad ibn Muhammad ibn Abī al-Fadl al-Harastānī informed us: Abū Muhammad Tāhir ibn Sahl ibn Bishr al-Isfarāyīnī informed us: Abū al-Husayn Muhammad ibn Bakr ibn ‘Uthmān al-Azdī informed us: Abū al-Hasan Ahmad ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn Zurayq informed us, from a selection made by Khalaf, the hadith master: Abū Muhammad ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hajjāj ibn Rishdīn al-Mahdī narrated to us, as it was read to him: Abū ‘Amr al-Hārith ibn Miskīn narrated to us: Sufyān ibn ‘Uyaynah narrated to us, from al-Zuhrī, from Sālim, from his father, that Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Kill snakes, including the two-striped and the short-tailed varieties, for these two destroy eyesight and cause miscarriages.”

Ibn ‘Umar used to kill every snake. Abū Lubābah—or Zayd ibn al-Khattāb—saw him pursuing a snake and said to him:

“Killing snakes that live in houses has been forbidden.”

---

## Page 9244

Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad al-Hārithī, Shaykh Shams al-Dīn ibn Abī ‘Umar, and Ahmad ibn Shaybān informed us of this hadith. They said:

Ibn Mulā‘ib informed us: al-Urmawī informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ibn al-Busrī informed us: Abū Ahmad al-Faradī informed us: Abū Bakr al-Mutayrī narrated to us: Bishr ibn Matar informed us: Sufyān narrated to us, and related the same hadith.

He was born in 609 AH and died in Cairo in Dhū al-Hijjah 684 AH.

Hadith Twenty-Nine:

Imam Shams al-Dīn Abū al-Faraj ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Malik ibn ‘Uthmān ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn Sa‘d al-Maqdisī informed us in 681 AH, as did Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān and Ismā‘īl ibn al-‘Asqalānī. The first two said:

Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd al-Kindī informed us.

The latter two said: Abū Hafs ibn Tabarzadh informed us.

They both said:

Judge Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Bāqī ibn Muhammad al-Ansārī informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Umar ibn al-Husayn ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad al-Khaffāf informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 447 AH: Abū al-Fadl ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad al-Zuhrī informed us, as it was read to him in 373 AH, saying: We were told by Muhammad

---

## Page 9245

ibn Hārūn, who said: Muhammad ibn Sulaymān ibn Habīb narrated to us: Sa‘īd ibn Rāshid narrated to us, from ‘Atā’, from Ibn ‘Umar, who reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Only the one who gave the call to prayer should give the call to commence it.”

He was born in 606 AH and died in Dhū al-Qa‘dah 689 AH.

Hadith Thirty:

The well-born hadith transmitter Najm al-Dīn Abū al-‘Izz Yūsuf ibn Ya‘qūb ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Alī al-Mujāwir al-Shaybānī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Muharram 680 AH, as did al-Muslim ibn ‘Allān. They both said:

Abū al-Yaman Zayd ibn al-Hasan ibn Zayd al-Kindī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Mansūr ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muhammad ibn Zurayq al-Qazzāz al-Shaybānī informed us: the hadith master Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn ‘Alī ibn Thābit al-Khatīb informed us: Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Husayn ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan al-Mu’addib informed us: ‘Alī ibn al-Hasan ibn al-Muthannā al-‘Anbarī narrated to me in Astarābādh: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Ja‘far ibn Sa‘īd al-Jawharī al-Baghdādī narrated to us in Arrajān: al-Hasan ibn ‘Arafah narrated to us.

Al-Khatīb said: Abū ‘Umar ibn Mahdī and a group of others also informed us. They said:

Ismā‘īl ibn Muhammad al-Saffār informed us: al-Hasan ibn ‘Arafah narrated to us: We were told by Ismā‘īl ibn

---

## Page 9246

‘Ayyāsh: Mūsā ibn ‘Uqbah narrated to us, from Nāfi‘, from Ibn ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with them both, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Neither a person in a state of major ritual impurity nor a menstruating woman may recite any part of the Quran.”

This is the wording of al-Jawharī’s narration. Al-Tirmidhī narrated it from Ibn ‘Arafah and Ibn Hajar, and Ibn Mājah narrated it from Hishām ibn ‘Ammār, all of them from Ismā‘īl.

Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Dā’im also informed us, through a chain with few intermediaries, as it was read to him: Abū al-Faraj ibn Kulayb informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ibn Bayān informed us: Abū al-Hasan ibn Makhlad informed us: al-Saffār informed us (and he related the hadith).

He was born in 601 AH and died in Dhū al-Qa‘dah 690 AH.

Hadith Thirty-One:

The shaykh, imam and hadith master Jamāl al-Dīn Abū Hāmid Muhammad ibn ‘Alī ibn Mahmūd ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Alī ibn al-Sābūnī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in Ramadan 668 AH: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd al-Samad ibn Muhammad ibn Abī al-Fadl informed us

---

## Page 9247

al-Harastānī, as it was read to him: Jamāl al-Islām Abū al-Hasan ‘Alī ibn al-Muslim ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Alī ibn al-Fath al-Sulamī informed us in 526 AH: Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Wāhid ibn Muhammad ibn Abī al-Hadīd informed us: Abū al-Hasan ‘Alī ibn Mūsā ibn al-Husayn informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Alī ibn Ya‘qūb ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Abī al-Sa‘b informed us: Abū Zur‘ah ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn ‘Amr ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn Safwān al-Basrī narrated to us: ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ibrāhīm narrated to us: al-Walīd ibn Muslim narrated to us, from al-Awzā‘ī, who said:

I asked al-Zuhrī about the woman who sought refuge from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He said: ‘Urwah informed me, from ‘Ā’ishah:

“When the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, brought the daughter of al-Jawn and drew near to her, she said, ‘I seek refuge in Allah from you.’ He said, ‘Return to your family’—a single pronouncement of divorce.”

Abū Zur‘ah said:

None of the leading scholars of hadith narrated it except al-Awzā‘ī.

He was born in 604 AH and died in Dhū al-Qa‘dah 680 AH.

Hadith Thirty-Two:

Al-Jamāl Ahmad ibn Abī Bakr ibn Sulaymān al-Wā‘iz ibn al-Hamawī informed us, through my reading to him while I listened, in Rajab 680 AH, and also as it was read to him in 681 AH:

---

## Page 9248

Abū Muhammad ‘Abd al-Jalīl ibn Abī Ghālib ibn Abī al-Ma‘ālī ibn Mandawayh informed us, as it was read to him while I listened, in 610 AH: Abū al-Mahāsin Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Naqqūr al-Bazzār informed us, as it was read to him: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Ubayd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Ishāq ibn Habbābah informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-‘Azīz al-Baghawī narrated to us in 315 AH: Abū ‘Uthmān Tālūt ibn ‘Abbād al-Sayrafī narrated to us from his book: Faddāl ibn Jubayr narrated to us: I heard Abū Umāmah al-Bāhilī say:

I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

“Guarantee me six things, and I will guarantee you Paradise: when any of you speaks, let him not lie; when entrusted with something, let him not betray that trust; and when he makes a promise, let him not break it. Lower your gaze, restrain your hands, and guard your chastity.”

He was born around 600 AH and died in Dhū al-Hijjah 687 AH.

Hadith Thirty-Three:

The trustworthy and truthful shaykh, Shams al-Dīn Abū Ghālib al-Muzaffar ibn ‘Abd al-Samad ibn Khalīl al-Ansārī, informed us, as it was read to him while I listened, in Jumādā al-Ākhirah 684 AH, as did Abū Muhammad ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Abbās al-Fāqūsī and Abū ‘Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Sulaymān al-‘Āmirī: We were informed by Qādī Abū al-Qāsim

---

## Page 9249

‘Abd al-Samad ibn Muhammad ibn Abī al-Fadl ibn al-Harastānī: Abū Muhammad Tāhir ibn Sahl ibn Bishr ibn Ahmad al-Isfarāyīnī informed us: Abū al-Husayn Muhammad ibn Makkī ibn ‘Uthmān ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Azdī al-Misrī informed us: Abū al-Hasan Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn al-‘Abbās al-Akhmīmī narrated to us, from material selected by ‘Abd al-Ghanī ibn Sa‘īd: Abū Ja‘far Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn Salāmah narrated to us: Yūnus ibn ‘Abd al-A‘lā narrated to us: ‘Abd Allāh ibn Wahb narrated to us: Talhah ibn Abī Sa‘īd narrated to me that Sa‘īd al-Maqburī narrated to him from Abū Hurayrah that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever keeps a horse for the cause of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, out of faith in Allah and belief in His promise, its food and drink, its dung and urine, will count as good deeds in his balance on the Day of Resurrection.”

He died in Jumādā al-Ūlā 688 AH at the age of eighty-two. Al-Fāqūsī died in Sha‘bān 682 AH at the age of seventy-five.

Hadith Thirty-Four:

The shaykh and imam, Muhyī al-Dīn Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Abī ‘Asrūn al-Tamīmī, informed us, as I read to him and listened, in 682 AH

---

## Page 9250

and Abū Hāmid al-Sābūnī. Both said:

Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd al-Samad ibn Muhammad ibn Abī al-Fadl al-Harastānī informed us: Abū Muhammad Tāhir ibn Sahl al-Isfarāyīnī informed us: Abū al-Husayn Muhammad ibn Makkī al-Azdī informed us: Judge Abū al-Husayn ‘Alī ibn Muhammad ibn Ishāq ibn Yazīd al-Halabī informed us in 390 AH: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd al-Samad ibn Sa‘īd, the judge, narrated to us: ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Jābir al-Kalā‘ī narrated to us: Yahyā ibn Sālih al-Wuhāzī narrated to us: al-‘Alā’ ibn Sulaymān narrated to us, from al-Zuhrī, from Abū Salamah, from Abū Hurayrah, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah does not take away knowledge by snatching it from people; rather, He takes away the scholars. When He leaves no scholar, people take ignorant leaders, who are asked for religious rulings and issue them without knowledge. Thus they go astray and lead others astray.”

Abū al-Hasan ibn al-Bukhārī also related it to us through a shorter chain of transmission: Ibn Tabarzadh informed us: Judge Abū Bakr informed us: ‘Alī ibn Ibrāhīm al-Bāqilānī informed us: Muhammad ibn Ismā‘īl al-Warrāq narrated to us by dictation: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Sulaymān al-Wāsitī narrated to us: Suwayd ibn Sa‘īd narrated to us: Mālik ibn Anas and Hafs ibn Maysarah narrated to us, from Hishām ibn ‘Urwah, from his father, from ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Amr (who then related the hadith).

---

## Page 9251

Al-Bukhārī and Muslim recorded it through Hishām. He was born in 599 AH and died on 3 Dhū al-Qa‘dah 682 AH.

Hadith 35:

The chief judge, Nafīs al-Dīn Abū al-Qāsim Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Alī ibn Jarīr al-Hārithī al-Shāfi‘ī, informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 679 AH, as did Shaykh Shams al-Dīn ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Abī ‘Umar and Ahmad ibn Shaybān.

They said:

Abū al-Barakāt Dāwūd ibn Ahmad ibn Mulā‘ib al-Baghdādī informed us, as it was read to him: Imam Abū al-Fadl Muhammad ibn ‘Umar ibn Yūsuf al-Urmawī informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 546 AH: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Alī ibn Ahmad ibn Muhammad ibn al-Busrī informed us in 465 AH: Abū Ahmad ‘Ubayd Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Abī Muslim al-Fardī informed us: Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Ja‘far ibn Ahmad al-Matīrī narrated to us in 333 AH: Abū Ahmad Bishr ibn Matar al-Wāsitī informed us in Sirr Man Ra’ā: Sufyān ibn ‘Uyaynah narrated to us, from al-Zuhrī, from Sālim, from his father:

---

## Page 9252

from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“There is no envy except in two cases: a man whom Allah has given the Quran and who recites it by night and by day, and a man whom Allah has given wealth and who spends it rightfully by night and by day.”

He died in Safar 680 AH at the age of seventy-three.

Hadith 36:

The shaykh, imam and ascetic Shams al-Dīn Abū ‘Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn al-Kamāl ‘Abd al-Rahīm ibn ‘Abd al-Wāhid ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Rahmān, and Shams al-Dīn ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn al-Zayn Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Malik, both al-Maqdisī, informed us, as it was read to them while I listened in 681 AH.

They said:

Al-Sharīf Abū al-Futūh Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Amrūn al-Bakrī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū al-As‘ad Hibat al-Rahmān ibn ‘Abd al-Wāhid ibn Abī al-Qāsim ‘Abd al-Karīm ibn Hawāzin al-Qushayrī informed us: My grandfather informed us: Abū al-Husayn al-Khaffāf informed us: Abū al-‘Abbās al-Sarrāj informed us: Qutaybah ibn Sa‘īd narrated to us: Al-Layth narrated to us, from Nāfi‘, from Ibn ‘Umar, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

---

## Page 9253

“One who misses the ‘Asr prayer is as though he has been bereft of his family and wealth.”

He was born in 607 AH and died in Jumādā al-Ūlā 688 AH.

Hadith 37:

The pious scholar Umm al-Khayr Sitt al-‘Arab bint Yahyā ibn Qāymāz ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Tājiyyah al-Kindiyyah informed us, as it was read to her while I listened in Ramadan 681 AH, as did Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān, Ibn al-‘Asqalānī and Abū al-Hasan ibn al-Bukhārī.

They said:

Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh informed us, as it was read to him while we listened: Abū Ghālib Ahmad ibn al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Bannā’ informed us, as it was read to him while I listened in 524 AH: Abū Muhammad al-Hasan ibn ‘Alī ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan al-Jawharī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Ja‘far ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī‘ī informed us: Muhammad ibn Yūnus ibn Mūsā narrated to us: Abū ‘Āsim al-Nabīl narrated to us, from Hanzalah ibn Abī Sufyān, from al-Qāsim, from ‘Ā’ishah, who reported:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to perform the ritual bath for major ritual impurity and would take

---

## Page 9254

a handful of water for the right side of his head, then take a handful for the left side of his head.” Recorded by al-Bukhārī, Muslim, Abū Dāwūd and al-Nasā’ī, from Abū Mūsā al-Zamin, from Abū ‘Āsim. She was born in 599 AH and died in 684 AH.

Hadith 38:

The distinguished scholar of noble lineage, Umm al-‘Arab Fātimah bint Abī al-Qāsim ‘Alī ibn Abī Muhammad al-Qāsim ibn Abī al-Qāsim ‘Alī ibn al-Hasan ibn Hibat Allāh ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Husayn ibn ‘Asākir, informed us, as it was read to her while I listened in Ramadan 681 AH, as did Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān and Sitt al-‘Arab bint Yahyā ibn Qāymāz.

They said:

Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh informed us, as it was read to him while we listened: Abū al-Qāsim Hibat Allāh ibn Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Wāhid ibn Ahmad ibn al-Husayn al-Shaybānī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Tālib Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ghaylān informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Ishāq Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn Yahyā al-Muzakkī al-Naysābūrī informed us, as it was read to him in 354 AH: Abū al-Qāsim Muhammad ibn

---

## Page 9255

Ishāq: Qutaybah ibn Sa‘īd narrated to us: Ja‘far ibn Sulaymān narrated to us, from Thābit, from Anas, who said:

“Rain fell while we were with the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He uncovered his head so that the rain fell upon it. I asked him, ‘Why did you do this, Messenger of Allah?’ He replied, ‘It has only just come from its Lord, Mighty and Majestic.’”

She was born in 598 AH and died in Sha‘bān 683 AH.

Hadith 39:

The pious, devoted and diligent worshipper, Umm Ahmad Zaynab bint Makkī ibn ‘Alī ibn Kāmil al-Harrānī, together with Ahmad ibn Shaybān, Ismā‘īl ibn al-‘Asqalānī and Fātimah bint ‘Alī ibn ‘Asākir, informed us, as it was read to them.

They said:

Abū Hafs ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Tabarzadh al-Baghdādī informed us: Abū Ghālib Ahmad ibn al-Hasan ibn Ahmad ibn al-Bannā’ informed us: Abū Muhammad al-Hasan ibn ‘Alī ibn Muhammad al-Jawharī informed us: Abū Bakr Ahmad ibn Ja‘far ibn Hamdān ibn Mālik al-Qatī‘ī informed us, as it was read to him: Abū Muslim Ibrāhīm ibn ‘Abd Allāh ibn

---

## Page 9256

Muslim al-Basrī narrated to us: Sulaymān ibn Harb narrated to us: Shu‘bah narrated to us, from ‘Adī ibn Thābit, who said: I heard al-Barā’ say:

“When Ibrāhīm, the son of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, died, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘He has a wet nurse in Paradise.’”

Al-Bukhārī narrated it from Salmān ibn Harb. She was born in 598 AH and died in Shawwāl 688 AH.

Hadith 40:

The pious scholar Umm Muhammad Zaynab bint Ahmad ibn ‘Umar ibn Kāmil al-Maqdisiyyah informed us, as it was read to her while I listened, in 684 AH, as did Abū ‘Abd Allāh ibn Badr, Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Shaybān, and Ibn al-‘Asqalānī.

They said: Ibn Tabarzadh informed us: Ibn al-Baydāwī, al-Qazzāz, and Ibn Yūsuf informed us. They said: Ibn al-Muslimah informed us: Al-Mukhallis informed us: Abū al-Qāsim ‘Abd Allāh ibn Muhammad informed us: Al-Hasan ibn Isrā’īl al-Nahrtīrī narrated to us: ‘Īsā ibn Yūnus narrated to us, from Usāmah ibn Zayd, from Sulaymān ibn Yasār, from Umm

---

## Page 9257

Salamah, the wife of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would reach dawn in a state of major ritual impurity, not resulting from a wet dream, and then complete his fast.”

She was born in 601 AH and died in Shawwāl 687 AH.

## Page 9258

Shaykh al-Islām was asked about the saying reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as relating that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:

“Neither My heaven nor My earth contains Me, but the heart of My believing servant contains Me.”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. This is among the Israelite traditions they have related. It has no known chain of transmission (*) from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Its meaning is: “His heart contains love for Me and knowledge of Me.”

As for the reported saying:

“The heart is the house of the Lord,”

it is of the same kind as the first, for the heart is the dwelling place of faith in Allah Most High, knowledge of Him, and love for Him.

As for what they relate:

“I was an unknown treasure, and I wished to be known, so I created creatures and made Myself known to them; thus, through Me, they came to know Me,”

these are not the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and I know of no chain of transmission for it, whether sound or weak.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah created the intellect and said to it, ‘Come forward,’ and it came forward. Then He said to it, ‘Turn back,’ and it turned back. Then He said, ‘By My might

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 52):
> On these pages, the Shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—mentioned a number of weak and fabricated hadiths. However, some textual corruption has occurred: in this fatwa, the expression wa-mā yarwūhu appears before each hadith, in twenty-five places. This is an obvious textual error that violates Arabic grammar. The correct wording is either wa-mā rawawhu (“and what they narrated”) or wa-mā yarwūnahu (“and what they narrate”), as is well known in Arabic. The latter is more likely correct, since this is the wording found in al-Kubrā, 2/231, which appears to be the source of this fatwa. And Allah Most High knows best.

and My majesty, I have created nothing nobler than you. Through you I take, and through you I give.’”

This hadith is false and fabricated, by the unanimous agreement of hadith scholars.

As for what they relate:

“Love of this world is the root of every sin,”

this is known from Jundub ibn Abd Allāh al-Bajalī. As a report from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, however, it has no known chain of transmission.

As for what they relate:

“This world is but one step for a believer,”

this is not known from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, nor from anyone else among the early generations of the Muslim community or its leading scholars.

As for what they relate:

“Whoever is granted blessing in something should hold fast to it; whoever binds himself to something is bound by it,”

the first statement is reported from some of the early Muslims. The second is false, for someone who binds himself to something may or may not be bound by it, depending on what Allah and His Messenger command.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Do favors for the poor, for tomorrow they will have their day—and what a day it will be!”

“Poverty is my pride, and in it I take pride,”

both are lies, and neither is known in any of the Muslims’ recognized books.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“I am the city of knowledge, and Alī is its gate,”

this hadith is weak—indeed, fabricated—according to hadith scholars.

## Page 9260

but al-Tirmidhī and others have transmitted it and attributed it to the Prophet, though it is a lie.

As for what they relate:

“The poor will sit on the Day of Resurrection, and He will say: ‘By My might and majesty, I did not withhold this world from you because you were insignificant to Me. Rather, I intended to raise your standing on this Day. Go to the place of gathering, and whoever treated you kindly by giving you a morsel of bread, a drink of water, or a piece of cloth to wear, take him to Paradise.’”

The shaykh said: The second report is a lie, transmitted by no hadith scholar. It is false and contradicts the Book, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus.

As for what they relate concerning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

and the rest of the poem. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to them: ‘Shake your sieves! May Allah bless you!’”

The report concerning women beating frame drums at celebrations is authentic; this took place during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

As for the saying:

“Shake your sieves!”

this is not known to have been said by him.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he said:

“O Allah, You have brought me out of the place most beloved to me, so settle me in the place most beloved to You.”

This

---

## Page 9261

is a baseless, fabricated hadith, though al-Tirmidhī and others have transmitted it. Rather, he said to Mecca:

“You are the most beloved of Allah’s lands to me.”

And he said:

“You are indeed the most beloved of lands to Allah.”

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever visits me and my father Abraham in the same year will enter Paradise.”

This is a fabricated lie, transmitted by none of the scholars of hadith.

As for what they relate from ʿAlī, may Allah be pleased with him, that a Bedouin pecked through his prayer, and ʿAlī told him, “Do not peck through your prayer,” whereupon the Bedouin replied, “O ʿAlī, had your father pecked through it, he would not have entered the Fire”—this is a lie.

As for what they relate about ʿUmar—that he killed his father—this is a lie, for his father died before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent as a messenger.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“I was a prophet when Adam was between water and clay.”

And:

“I was a prophet when Adam was neither water nor clay.”

This wording is false and baseless.

As for what they relate:

“The unmarried man’s bed is made of fire. Pitiable is a man without a wife, and pitiable is a woman without a husband.”

These are not the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

---

## Page 9262

Nor is the following account established:

“When Abraham, Allah’s close friend, peace be upon him, built the House, he prayed a thousand rakʿahs at each corner. Allah Most High then revealed to him: ‘O Abraham, this neither relieves hunger nor covers nakedness.’”

This is an obvious lie, found in none of the Muslims’ books.

As for what they relate:

“Do not hate tribulation, for through it the hypocrites are cut down.”

This is not known to have been reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

As for what they relate:

“Whoever teaches his brother a verse from Allah’s Book owns him as a slave.”

This is a lie, found in none of the scholars’ books.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“I surveyed the sins of my community and found no one guilty of a greater sin than someone who learned a verse and then forgot it.”

If this hadith is authentic, then forgetting here means forgetting its recitation. The wording of the hadith is that he said:

“Among the sins of my community is that a man is given a verse of the Quran by Allah, but sleeps through it until he forgets it.”

Forgetting can mean turning away from the Quran and abandoning belief in it and acting upon it. As for neglecting to review it until it is forgotten, that is a sin.

As for what they relate:

“A verse of the Quran is better than Muhammad and the family of Muhammad. The Quran is Allah’s speech, sent down and uncreated, and is not to be likened to anything else.”

The wording cited has not been transmitted.

---

## Page 9263

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever possesses beneficial knowledge and conceals it from the Muslims will be bridled by Allah with a bridle of fire on the Day of Resurrection.”

Its meaning is known from the Sunan collections, in which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever is asked about knowledge he possesses and conceals it will be bridled by Allah with a bridle of fire on the Day of Resurrection.”

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“When you come to discuss the disputes that arose among my Companions, refrain; and when you come to discuss divine decree and predestination, refrain.”

This has been transmitted through disconnected chains of transmission.

As for the report that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to Salmān al-Fārisī, while he was eating grapes:

“Daw, daw”—meaning, “Two grapes at a time.”

These are not the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; the report is false.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“If a man commits fornication with a woman and she bears him a daughter, he may marry his daughter born of fornication.”

This is a view expressed by people outside al-Shāfiʿī’s school, some of whom attribute it to al-Shāfiʿī. Some of al-Shāfiʿī’s followers denied this attribution, saying that he did not explicitly declare it permissible. He did, however, explicitly permit the corresponding case involving kinship through breastfeeding, where milk is suckled from a woman pregnant as a result of fornication. Scholars generally, including Ahmad, Abū Hanīfah and others, agree that this is forbidden. This is also the stronger of the two positions in Mālik’s school.

## Page 9264

As for what they relate:

“The Book of Allah is that for which you are most entitled to take payment.”

Yes, it is established that he said:

“The Book of Allah is that for which you are most entitled to take payment.”

However, this occurs in the hadith concerning healing through recitation, and the payment was for the recovery of the people’s sick companion, not for the recitation itself.

Is it forbidden to keep dovecotes if the pigeons fly out of them, land on people’s crops and eat the grain? Is keeping dovecotes in villages and towns forbidden for this reason? Yes, if it harms people, it must be prevented.

As for what they relate from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever wrongs a dhimmī (a non-Muslim under Muslim protection), Allah will be his adversary on the Day of Resurrection”—or, “I will be his adversary on the Day of Resurrection.”

This is weak. What is known to have been reported from him, however, is that he said:

“Whoever kills a person under a covenant of protection without right will not smell the fragrance of Paradise.”

As for what they relate from him:

“Whoever lights a lamp in a mosque, the angels and the bearers of the Throne will continue to seek forgiveness for him as long as that lamp illuminates the mosque.”

I know of no chain of transmission for this from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

## Page 9265

Shaykh al-Islām was asked about the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reporting from his Lord, Mighty and Majestic:

“I have never hesitated over anything I am going to do as I hesitate over taking the soul of My believing servant: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress.”

“What is meant by Allah’s hesitation?”

He answered:

This is a noble hadith, narrated by al-Bukhārī on the authority of Abū Hurayrah. It is the most excellent hadith narrated concerning the qualities of Allah’s close friends. Some have rejected these words, saying: “Allah cannot be described as hesitating. Only someone who does not know how things will turn out hesitates, whereas Allah knows their outcomes best.”

Some of them have also said: “Allah is treated as one who hesitates.”

The truth of the matter is that the words of His Messenger are true. No one knows Allah better than His Messenger; no one is more sincere in counselling the Muslim community, more eloquent, or clearer in expression. This being so, anyone who quibbles and objects to him is among the most misguided, ignorant and ill-mannered of people. Indeed, he must be disciplined and given a discretionary punishment. The words of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, must be safeguarded

---

## Page 9266

from false suppositions and corrupt beliefs. Yet even if one of us hesitates over a matter because he does not know its outcome, what Allah has described Himself with is not comparable to what any one of us is described with. For there is nothing like Allah, whether in His essence, His attributes, or His actions. Moreover, that premise is itself false.

One of us sometimes hesitates because he does not know the consequences, and sometimes because of the benefits and harms involved in the two courses of action. He desires an action for its benefit and dislikes it for its harm, not because he is ignorant of it. One and the same thing may be loved in one respect and disliked in another, as the poet said:

Gray hair I dislike, yet I dislike parting with it too;
Marvel at something loved despite being hated!

This is like a sick person wanting his unpleasant medicine. Indeed, all the righteous deeds a servant wishes to perform despite his soul’s aversion to them fall into this category. An authentic hadith states:

“Hellfire is surrounded by desires, and Paradise is surrounded by hardships.”

Allah Most High says:

“Fighting has been prescribed for you, though it is hateful to you.”

And so the verse continues.

This makes clear the meaning of the hesitation mentioned in this hadith, for He said:

“My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him.”

The servant whose condition is such has become beloved to the True Lord and loves Him in return, drawing near to Him first through the obligatory duties, which He

---

## Page 9267

loves, and then striving diligently in the voluntary acts which He loves and whose performers He loves. Thus, the servant did all he could of what the True Lord loves. The True Lord therefore loved him for doing what He loves, the aim on both sides being an accord of wills, such that each loves what his beloved loves and dislikes what his beloved dislikes. The Lord dislikes causing distress to His servant and beloved. It follows that the servant dislikes death, so that he may do more of what his Beloved loves.

Yet Allah, glorified and exalted be He, has decreed death. Whatever He decrees, He wills, and it must inevitably occur. The Lord therefore wills His servant’s death in accordance with His prior decree, while nevertheless disliking the distress it causes His servant—the distress he experiences through death. Death is thus willed by the True Lord in one respect and disliked by Him in another. This is the reality of hesitation: one and the same thing is willed in one respect and disliked in another, even though one side must prevail, as the will for death prevails here, while the dislike of causing His servant distress remains. His willing the death of the believer whom He loves and dislikes distressing is not like His willing the death of the disbeliever whom He hates and wishes to distress.

He then said, after some remarks already quoted:

In this same category fall the disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience that occur in existence. Allah Most High hates these things, is displeased with them, dislikes them, and forbids them. Yet He, glorified be He, has ordained, decreed, and willed them by His universal will, even if He does not will them by His religious will. This is the decisive answer to the question over which people have disputed: whether He, glorified be He, commands what He does not will.

---

## Page 9268

The prevalent view among the affirmationist theologians and the jurists who agree with them is that He commands what He does not will. The Qadariyyah, the Mu‘tazilah, and others, however, say that He commands only what He wills.

The precise distinction is that will in the Book of Allah is of two kinds: a religious, legislative will and a universal, decretive will. Examples of the first include His words, Most High:

“Allah desires ease for you and does not desire hardship for you.”

And His words, Most High:

“But He wishes to purify you.”

And His words, Most High:

“Allah wishes to make things clear to you and guide you to the ways of those before you,”

through to His words:

“And Allah wishes to accept your repentance.”

Here, will means love and approval; this is the religious will. This is also indicated by His words:

“I did not create jinn and mankind except to worship Me.”

As for the universal, decretive will, an example is His saying, Most High:

“Whomever Allah wills to guide, He opens his heart to Islam; and whomever He wills to lead astray, He makes his heart tight and constricted, as though he were climbing into the sky.”

Another example is the Muslims’ saying: “Whatever Allah wills comes to pass, and whatever He does not will does not come to pass.” Everything that occurs falls within this will and volition: neither good nor evil, neither right nor wrong, falls outside it. This will and volition encompass things that the legislative command does not encompass. The religious will, however, corresponds exactly to the legislative command; the two never differ. The same distinction found in the term “will” also applies to the terms “command,” “words,” and “judgment”

---

## Page 9269

, as well as “decree,” “writing,” “sending forth,” “sending,” and similar terms. All of these have both a universal, decretive sense and a religious, legislative sense.

The universal words are those from which neither the righteous nor the wicked can escape. These are the words through which the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, sought protection when he said:

“I seek refuge in the perfect words of Allah, whose bounds neither the righteous nor the wicked can overstep.”

Allah Most High says:

“His command, when He wills a thing, is only to say to it, ‘Be,’ and it is.”

The religious words, by contrast, are the revealed scriptures, concerning which the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever fights so that the word of Allah is supreme is fighting in Allah’s cause.”

And He Most High says:

“And she believed in the words of her Lord and His scriptures.”

Likewise, the religious command is exemplified by His words, Most High:

“Indeed, Allah commands you to return trusts to their rightful owners.”

The universal command is exemplified by:

“His command, when He wills a thing…”

Sending forth in the religious sense is exemplified by His words, Most High:

“It is He who sent forth among the unlettered a Messenger from among themselves.”

Sending forth in the universal sense is exemplified by:

“We sent forth against you servants of Ours.”

Sending in the religious sense is exemplified by His words:

“It is He who sent His Messenger with guidance and the religion of truth.”

And in the universal sense:

“Do you not see that We have sent the devils against the disbelievers, vehemently inciting them?”

---

## Page 9270

This is discussed at length elsewhere. The evil deeds that occur in existence are willed by Allah through His universal will and fall within His words whose bounds neither the righteous nor the wicked can overstep. Nevertheless, He, glorified be He, has not willed them through His religious will, nor do they accord with His religious words. He does not approve of disbelief for His servants, nor does He command indecency. Thus, in one respect, these deeds are displeasing to Him.

Yet these are not like taking the believer’s soul. He dislikes that—what He dislikes is causing the believer distress—yet He wills it because He has already decreed death for him, so it is inevitable. What He wills for His believing servant is good for him and a mercy to him, for it is authentically reported:

“Allah Most High decrees nothing for the believer except that it is good for him. If prosperity comes his way, he gives thanks, and that is good for him; if adversity befalls him, he remains patient, and that is good for him.”

As for evil deeds, He hates and dislikes them. In this respect, they have no praiseworthy outcome unless those who commit them repent and are shown mercy through repentance, although repentance must necessarily be preceded by a sin.

Accordingly, the question of sins being decreed for the believer is answered in two ways:

First: this hadith does not encompass sins; it concerns only afflictions.

Second: if he repents of them, what is followed by repentance becomes good, for repentance is a good deed, indeed one of the deeds most beloved to Allah. Allah rejoices at His servant’s repentance, when he turns to Him, with the greatest possible joy. As for sins from which a person does not repent, they are evil for the one who commits them. Allah, glorified be He, has ordained and decreed everything on account of His

---

## Page 9271

wisdom, as He says:

“The handiwork of Allah, who has perfected all things.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Who perfected everything He created.”

Thus, Allah has a wise purpose in every created thing. Yet this is a vast subject, which we have discussed at length elsewhere. The point here is to draw attention to the fact that a particular thing may be loved in one respect and disliked in another, and that this is the true nature of hesitation. Just as this applies to actions, it also applies to persons. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9272

Shaykh al-Islām was asked about the meaning of the hadith narrated by Abū Dharr—may Allah be pleased with him—from the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—reporting that Allah, Blessed and Exalted, said:

“O My servants, I have forbidden injustice for Myself and have made it forbidden among you, so do not wrong one another.

O My servants, all of you are astray except those whom I guide, so seek guidance from Me and I shall guide you.

O My servants, all of you are hungry except those whom I feed, so ask Me for food and I shall feed you.

O My servants, all of you are naked except those whom I clothe, so ask Me for clothing and I shall clothe you.

O My servants, you sin by night and by day, and I forgive all sins, so seek My forgiveness and I shall forgive you.

O My servants, you can never harm Me, nor can you ever benefit Me.

O My servants, if all of you, from the first to the last, humans and jinn alike, were as pious in heart as the most pious man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the slightest.

O My servants, if all of you, from the first to the last, humans and jinn alike, were as wicked in heart as the most wicked man among you, that would not diminish My dominion in the slightest.

O My servants, if all of you, from the first to the last, humans and jinn alike, stood together on a single plain and asked of Me, and I granted each person what he asked for, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when dipped into it.

O My servants, it is only your deeds that I record for you; then I shall recompense you in full

---

## Page 9273

for them. Whoever finds good should praise Allah, the Almighty and Majestic, and whoever finds otherwise should blame no one but himself.”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. There is no power or strength except through Allah.

As for His saying, Exalted is He, “O My servants, I have forbidden injustice for Myself,” it raises two major questions, each with many aspects and ramifications.

The first concerns the injustice that Allah has forbidden for Himself and declared Himself free of in His words:

“We did not wrong them.”

“Your Lord wrongs no one.”

“Your Lord is never unjust to His servants.”

“Indeed, Allah does not wrong anyone even by an atom’s weight; and if there is a good deed, He multiplies it.”

“Say: The enjoyment of this world is brief, and the Hereafter is better for those who fear Allah; and you will not be wronged even as much as the thread of a date stone.”

He has also denied that He wills injustice, saying:

“Allah wills no injustice to the worlds.”

“Allah wills no injustice to His servants.”

He has also assured His servants that they need not fear it, saying:

“Whoever does righteous deeds while being a believer need fear neither injustice nor deprivation.”

People have differed over the meaning of this injustice, taking positions at two widely separated extremes, with a middle course between them—and the best course lies in the middle. This arose from discussion of divine decree and its compatibility with the revealed law. Delving into this without thorough knowledge has led the great majority of nations astray. For this reason, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—forbade his Companions to dispute about it.

---

## Page 9274

Thus, the deniers of divine decree among the Mu'tazilah and others—who maintain that Allah did not create His servants’ acts and did not will anything to occur except what He commanded—together with their extremists, who deny Allah’s prior knowledge and recording of His servants’ future acts, held that injustice on His part would be like the injustice human beings commit against one another. They likened His acts to those of His servants, thus becoming likeners of divine acts to human acts. They drew comparisons for Allah rather than ascribing to Him the highest description. Indeed, they declared things obligatory or forbidden for Him according to what they considered obligatory or forbidden for His servants, drawing an analogy between Him and His servants and establishing the ruling in the original case through personal opinion.

On this basis, they said: “If He commands a servant but does not assist him with every form of assistance within His power, He wrongs him.” They committed themselves to the conclusion that He cannot guide someone who is astray, just as they said that He cannot lead a rightly guided person astray. They also said: “If He gives two people the same command but singles out one of them for assistance in carrying it out, He is unjust.” They said similar things about other matters of grace and kindness, treating His withholding of them as injustice.

Likewise, they supposed that punishing someone whose act had been decreed would be an injustice to him. They failed to distinguish between punishing someone in whom the grounds for deserving punishment were present and someone in whom they were not—even if He created that state of deserving punishment for another wise purpose, whether general or particular.

Here, feet have slipped and understandings have gone astray. Others among the speculative theologians who affirm divine decree opposed them, saying:

“Injustice on His part has no reality

---

## Page 9275

that could possibly exist; rather, it is intrinsically impossible. It therefore cannot fall within His power, nor can it be said that He refrains from it by His choice and will. It belongs instead to the same category as combining two opposites, placing a single body in two locations, or turning the beginningless into something originated and the originated into something beginningless. Otherwise, whatever can be conceived of, whose existence is possible and which Allah has the power to bring about, is not injustice on His part, whether He does it or not.”

Various groups among the affirmationist jurists and hadith scholars—including followers of Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, Ahmad and others, as well as hadith commentators and their like—adopted this position from them. They interpreted this hadith on that basis. Sometimes they appealed to the apparent meaning of transmitted statements, such as the report we have narrated from Iyās ibn Mu‘āwiyah, who said:

“I have never debated anyone with the full force of my reason except the Qadariyyah. I asked them, ‘What is injustice?’

“They replied, ‘Taking what does not belong to you, or disposing of what does not belong to you.’

“I said, ‘But everything belongs to Allah.’”

Iyās intended only to show that the acts in question concern what Allah owns, and therefore cannot constitute injustice according to their own definition. This is undisputed among the affirmationists, for they agree with those who believe in divine decree that everything Allah does is just.

In the hadith concerning distress, reported by Imam Ahmad from ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No servant is ever afflicted by worry or sorrow and says: ‘O Allah, I am Your servant, the son of Your servant and the son of Your maidservant. My forelock is in Your hand

---

## Page 9276

; Your judgment concerning me takes effect, and Your decree concerning me is just. I ask You by every name belonging to You—by which You have named Yourself, which You have revealed in Your Book, which You have taught to any of Your creation, or which You have kept to Yourself in the knowledge of the unseen with You—to make the Quran the springtime of my heart, the light of my breast, the dispeller of my sorrow, and the remover of my worry and distress’—but Allah removes his worry and distress and replaces them with joy.

“They asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, should we not learn these words?’

“He replied, ‘Indeed! Whoever hears them should learn them.’”

He thus made clear that every decree of His concerning His servant is just. Hence it is said: “Every blessing from Him is grace, and every punishment from Him is justice.”

It is also said: “I obeyed You by Your grace, and all favor belongs to You. I disobeyed You with Your knowledge—or by Your justice—and Yours is the case against me. I therefore ask You, by the binding force of Your case against me and my lack of any defense, to forgive me.”

This exchange involving Iyās resembles what Rabī‘ah ibn Abī ‘Abd al-Rahmān said to Ghaylān when Ghaylān asked him, “I ask you by Allah: do you think Allah loves to be disobeyed?”

He replied, “I ask you by Allah: do you think Allah is disobeyed by force?”—meaning, by overpowering Him. It was as though he had stopped his mouth with a stone.

The expression “loves to be disobeyed” is ambiguous. In a debate, it may not be feasible to explain ambiguous expressions for fear of an opponent’s obstinate disputation, so one resorts to clear statements. Thus he said, “Do you then think He is disobeyed by force?” This forces his opponent to accept the implication that Allah is powerless—a necessary consequence of the position of the Qadariyyah and of those worse than them, such as the eternalist philosophers and others. Likewise, Iyās saw that this answer, which accorded with their own definition, refuted them, and he did not enter into a lengthy discussion of the details.

---

## Page 9277

In sum, concerning His saying, Exalted is He:

“Whoever does righteous deeds while being a believer will fear neither injustice nor deprivation.”

The Quran commentators among the early generations said: He does not fear being wronged by having the sins of others laid upon him, nor being deprived by having his good deeds diminished. This injustice cannot mean something impossible and beyond His power, for the meaning would then be: he does not fear what is intrinsically impossible, outside the realm of possibility and beyond the scope of power.

For if such a thing cannot possibly exist—to the point that they say it is beyond His power even if He willed it, like creating an equal to Himself—how can its existence be conceived, let alone any fear of it be imagined, such that this fear should be negated? Moreover, what benefit is there in denying fear of such a thing? The context clearly shows that the intended meaning is that this person who does good will not be repaid for his goodness with injustice or deprivation. Thus, the injustice and deprivation being denied concern recompense, as the Quran commentators explained, and Allah recompenses him only according to his deeds.

Hence, the correct position, supported by the revealed texts, is that Allah punishes in the Hereafter only those who have sinned. As He says:

“I will surely fill Hell with you and all those among them who follow you.”

If anyone other than his followers entered it, it would not be filled with them alone. Accordingly, it is established in the two Sahīh collections, in the hadith about the dispute between Paradise and Hell, narrated by Abū Hurayrah and Anas:

“Hell will not be filled with those cast into it until it contracts upon itself and says, ‘Enough! Enough!’ after having said, ‘Is there any more?’ As for Paradise, space will remain beyond what is occupied by those who enter it from the people of this world, so Allah will bring another creation into being for it.”

---

## Page 9278

Hence, the correct position upheld by the leading scholars concerning those who were not held accountable in this world, such as the children of polytheists and others like them, is what the authentic hadith states: Allah knows best what they would have done. We therefore do not judge that all of them will enter Paradise, nor that all of them will enter Hell. Rather, they fall into different categories according to what becomes manifest of His knowledge when they are charged with obligations on the plains of the Resurrection, as the transmitted reports state.

Likewise, His saying, Exalted is He:

“Whoever does good, it is to his own benefit; and whoever does evil, it is to his own detriment. Your Lord is never unjust to His servants.”

The wording indicates that He does not wrong someone who does good by diminishing his good deeds or crediting them to someone else, nor does He wrong someone who does evil by burdening him with another’s sins. Rather, each soul receives the good it has earned and bears the evil it has earned.

This is like His saying:

“Or has he not been informed of what is in the Scriptures of Moses, and of Abraham, who fulfilled his obligations: that no bearer of burdens will bear the burden of another, and that a person will have only what he has striven for?”

He thus declares that no one bears any of another’s burden of sin, and that a person is entitled only to what he has striven for. Both statements are true in their plain meaning.

Some people may think that the deceased’s suffering because of his family’s weeping over him contradicts the first statement, but this is not so. The person who wails is punished for his own wailing; the deceased is not made to bear his sin. Rather, the deceased experiences pain because of this person’s actions, just as a person suffers from things beyond his own doing, even when that suffering is not recompense for his deeds. Suffering is broader in meaning than punishment, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Travel is a portion of suffering.”

---

## Page 9279

Likewise, some people have thought that the deceased’s benefiting from bodily acts of worship performed by the living contradicts His saying:

“And that a person will have only what he has striven for.”

But this is not so. In relation to this verse, the deceased’s benefiting from bodily acts of worship performed by the living is no different from his benefiting from acts of worship involving wealth. Anyone who claims that the verse contradicts one but not the other makes a plainly unsound claim.
