---

## Page 9450

nor do we testify that Allah loves him.”

Another group said that, on the contrary, we testify that anyone whose faith and piety are widely known among the people, and whom the Muslims unanimously praise, will enter Paradise. Such people include ‘Umar ibn ‘Abd al-‘Azīz, al-Hasan al-Basrī, Sufyān al-Thawrī, Abū Hanīfah, Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, Ahmad, al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād, Abū Sulaymān al-Dārānī, Ma‘rūf al-Karkhī, ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak—may Allah Most High be pleased with them—and others. For it is related in al-Sahīh:

“A funeral procession passed by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the people spoke well of the deceased. He said, ‘It is assured; it is assured.’ Another funeral procession passed by him, and the people spoke ill of the deceased. He said, ‘It is assured; it is assured.’ They asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, what do you mean by saying, “It is assured; it is assured”?’ He replied, ‘You spoke well of the first deceased person, so I said that Paradise was assured for them. You spoke ill of the second deceased person, so I said that the Fire was assured for them.’ He was asked, ‘On what grounds, O Messenger of Allah?’ He replied, ‘On account of the good and bad things said about them.’”

Once this is understood, many of those renowned as shaykhs in these times may have such ignorance, misguidance, disobedience, and sins as to prevent people from bearing such testimony on their behalf. Indeed, among them there may be hypocrites and evildoers, just as among them are Allah’s pious friends, His righteous servants, and His successful party. Such people are likewise found among those who are not shaykhs—and these are in Paradise—such as merchants, farmers, and people of other occupations.

---

## Page 9451

Since this is so, whoever seeks to be gathered with a shaykh whose ultimate fate he does not know is misguided. Rather, he must take what he knows as his basis and ask Allah to gather him with His Prophet and His righteous servants. As Allah Most High says:

“If you both support one another against him, then Allah is his Protector, as are Gabriel and the righteous believers.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Your only allies are Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe—those who establish prayer, give zakah, and bow in worship.”

“Whoever takes Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe as allies—indeed, it is Allah’s party that will prevail.”

Accordingly, whoever loves a shaykh who contravenes the Sharia will be with him; if that shaykh is admitted to the Fire, he will be with him. It is well known that shaykhs who contravene the Book and the Sunnah are people of misguidance and ignorance. Whoever is with them will therefore share the fate of the people of misguidance and ignorance.

As for Allah’s pious friends, such as Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, ‘Uthmān, ‘Alī, and others, loving them is among the firmest bonds of faith and the greatest good deeds of the pious. If someone loves a man because of the goodness apparent to him that Allah and His Messenger love, Allah Most High will reward him for loving what Allah and His Messenger love, even if he does not know that man’s true inward state. The foundation is love of Allah and love of what Allah loves. Whoever loves Allah and loves what Allah loves is one of Allah’s friends.

Yet many people profess this love without truly fulfilling it. Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you and forgive you your sins.’”

Some of the early Muslims said: “During the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, some people claimed that they

---

## Page 9452

loved Allah, so Allah revealed this verse.”

Love of Allah, His Messenger, and His pious servants entails doing what He loves and abandoning what He dislikes. People differ enormously in this regard: the greater a person’s share of this, the higher his rank with Allah.

As for someone who loves another out of personal desire—for some worldly benefit he receives from him, some need that person meets for him, money he obtains through him, partisan attachment to him, or similar reasons—this is not love for Allah’s sake. Rather, it is love driven by personal desire. This is the love that leads those who harbour it into disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience.

How many people claim to love shaykhs for Allah’s sake! Had they truly loved them for Allah’s sake, they would have obeyed Allah, for whose sake they loved them. Love for someone who is loved for another’s sake is subordinate to love for that other. How can someone who does not love Allah love anyone for Allah’s sake? And how can someone who turns away from the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and from Allah’s path be someone who loves Allah?

How many people love shaykhs, kings, and others, taking them as rivals to Allah and loving them as they love Allah! The distinction between loving for Allah’s sake and loving alongside Allah is clear. Those who associate partners with Allah take rivals whom they love as they love Allah, whereas the believers are stronger in their love for Allah.

The people of faith love, for the foundation of their love is love of Allah. Whoever loves Allah loves those whom Allah loves, and whoever is loved by Allah loves Allah. Thus, the beloved of the beloved is beloved to Allah and loves Allah. Whoever loves Allah is loved by Allah, and therefore loves those who love Allah.

---

## Page 9453

As for those who associate partners with Allah, they take rivals and intercessors whom they invoke besides Allah. Allah Most High says:

“You have come to Us alone, just as We created you the first time, leaving behind all that We bestowed upon you. We do not see with you those intercessors whom you claimed to be Allah’s partners in your affairs. The bonds between you have been severed, and all that you claimed has vanished from you.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Why should I not worship the One who created me, and to whom you will be returned?”

“Should I take other gods besides Him? If the Most Merciful intends harm for me, their intercession will avail me nothing, nor can they rescue me.”

“Then I would surely be in manifest error.”

“Indeed, I have believed in your Lord, so listen to me.”

Allah Most High says:

“Warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord, with no protector or intercessor besides Him, so that they may become mindful of Him.”

Allah Most High also says:

“It is not for any human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, judgment, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be my worshippers instead of Allah’s.’ Rather, ‘Be devoted to the Lord through your teaching of the Scripture and through your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and the prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have submitted to Allah?”

Allah Most High sent the messengers and revealed the Scriptures so that all worship would be devoted to Allah alone. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said in an authentic hadith:

“We prophets share one religion.”

Thus, the religion is one, even though the prescribed laws and ways differ. Allah Most High says:

“We never sent a messenger before you without revealing to him: ‘There is no god but Me, so worship Me.’”

Allah Most High also says:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: Did We appoint gods besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?”

Allah Most High says: “We certainly sent a messenger to every nation

---

## Page 9454

saying, ‘Worship Allah and shun false gods.’”

Ever since Allah sent Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—He accepts from anyone whom the message has reached only the religion with which He sent him, for his call extends to all creation. Allah Most High says:

“We have sent you only to all humanity.”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Any Jew or Christian of this community who hears of me and then does not believe in me will enter the Fire.”

Allah Most High says:

“My mercy encompasses all things. I shall ordain it for those who are mindful of Me, give zakat, and believe in Our signs.”

“Those who follow the Messenger, the unlettered Prophet, whom they find described in the Torah and the Gospel they possess. He enjoins upon them what is right and forbids them what is wrong, makes good things lawful for them and forbids them foul things, and relieves them of their burdens and the shackles that bound them. Those who believe in him, honour him, support him, and follow the light sent down with him—it is they who will prosper.”

“Say, ‘O humanity! I am Allah’s Messenger to you all. To Him belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth. There is no god but Him; He gives life and causes death. So believe in Allah and His Messenger, the unlettered Prophet who believes in Allah and His words, and follow him so that you may be guided.’”

All creation must therefore follow Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—worshipping none but Allah and worshipping Him according to the law of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and no other. Allah Most High says:

“Then We set you upon a clear path in matters of religion, so follow it, and do not follow the desires of those who do not know.”

“They will be of no help to you against Allah. Indeed, some of the wrongdoers

---

## Page 9455

are allies of one another, while Allah is the ally of those mindful of Him.”

They must unite upon this and not divide, as is established in an authentic report from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—who said:

“Allah is pleased with three things for you: that you worship Him without associating anything with Him; that you all hold firmly to the rope of Allah and do not divide; and that you offer sincere counsel to those whom Allah has placed in charge of your affairs.”

Worship of Allah entails complete love for Allah and complete humility before Him. The foundation and basis of religion is that Allah alone should be the One worshipped, whom hearts love and fear, and that they should have no deity besides Him. A “deity” is that which hearts worship with love, reverence, hope, fear, veneration, exaltation, and the like.

Allah, glorified and exalted is He, sent the messengers with the message that there is no deity but Him. Thus, through love for Him and hope in Him, hearts are freed from love for anything besides Him; through asking Him, from asking anything besides Him; through acting for Him, from acting for anything besides Him; and through seeking His help, from seeking help from anything besides Him. This is why the middle of al-Fātihah is:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and his family and grant them peace—said in an authentic hadith that Allah Most High says:

“I have divided the prayer between Myself and My servant. When he says, ‘The Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,’ He says, ‘My servant has extolled Me.’ When he says, ‘Master of the Day of Judgment,’ He says, ‘My servant has glorified Me.’ When he says, ‘You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help,’ He says, ‘This verse is divided into two halves between Myself and My servant, and My servant shall have what he asks for.’ And when he says, ‘Guide us to the straight path’

---

## Page 9456

‘the path of those You have blessed, not those who have incurred Your wrath, nor those who have gone astray,’ He says, ‘These are for My servant, and My servant shall have what he asks for.’”

Thus, the middle of the surah is:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

Religion, then, consists in worshipping Allah alone and seeking help from Him alone. The angels, the prophets, and all others are servants of Allah. As Allah Most High says:

“The Messiah would never disdain to be a servant of Allah, nor would the angels brought near to Him. Whoever disdains to worship Him and is arrogant—He will gather them all before Him.

“As for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will give them their rewards in full and grant them more from His bounty. But those who are disdainful and arrogant, He will punish with a painful punishment, and they will find neither protector nor helper besides Allah.”

Love for other than Allah is exemplified by the Christians’ love for the Messiah, the Jews’ love for Moses, the Rāfidah’s love for ‘Alī, and the extremists’ love for their shaykhs and imams. An example is someone who gives his allegiance to a shaykh or imam and turns others away from his counterpart, although the two are close or equal in rank. This is akin to the conduct of the People of the Book who believed in some messengers and disbelieved in others; to that of the Rāfidah, who give their allegiance to some Companions and show hostility towards others; and to that of partisans among those associated with jurisprudence and asceticism, who give their allegiance to some shaykhs and imams but not others. The true believer, however, is one who gives his allegiance to all people of faith.

Allah Most High says:

“The believers are but brothers.”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“One believer is to another like a building, each part strengthening the other”—and he interlaced his fingers.

And he said: “The likeness of

---

## Page 9457

believers in their mutual love and compassion is that of a body: when one part suffers, the rest of the body responds with fever and sleeplessness.” He—peace be upon him—also said:

“Do not sever ties with one another or turn your backs on one another. Be brothers, O servants of Allah.”

The distinction between love for Allah’s sake and love for other motives is further illustrated by Abū Bakr—may Allah be pleased with him—who loved the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—with sincere devotion to Allah. His uncle Abū Tālib, however, loved and supported him out of personal inclination, not for Allah’s sake. Allah therefore accepted Abū Bakr’s deeds and revealed concerning him:

“But the most God-fearing will be kept away from it—he who gives his wealth to purify himself, not to repay anyone for a favor done to him, but seeking the Face of his Lord, the Most High. And he will surely be pleased.”

As for Abū Tālib, Allah did not accept his deeds. Abū Bakr sought neither recompense nor reward from any created being—not from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—nor from anyone else. Rather, he believed in him, loved him, protected him, and supported him with his person and his wealth, seeking thereby to draw near to Allah and seeking reward from Allah. His Messenger conveys Allah’s commands and prohibitions, His promise and His warning. Allah Most High says:

“Your duty is only to convey the message, and Ours is to call to account.”

Allah alone creates and provides, gives and withholds, lowers and raises, grants honor and brings humiliation. He—glory be to Him—is the One who brings all causes into being, the Lord and Sovereign of everything. Among the means that people employ are those Allah has commanded or permitted; these may be pursued. Others He has prohibited outright, or they are religious innovations for which Allah has given no permission; these must not be pursued. Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim

---

## Page 9458

besides Allah.’ They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.”

“Intercession is of no avail with Him except for those to whom He grants permission.”

He—glory be to Him—has explained the error of those who invoke created beings, whether angels, prophets, or others. He made clear that created beings do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth; then He made clear that they have no share in either; then He made clear that He has no helper or supporter among them.

For those who associate partners with Allah liken the Creator to created beings. Some of them say: “If you have a need, call upon Shaykh so-and-so, and you will find him; or take steps toward his tomb and call out, ‘O Shaykh!’ Your need will be fulfilled.” This is an error, and doing so is impermissible. Even if some of those who invoke others besides Allah occasionally see the likeness of the one they invoke, that is a devil appearing to them in his likeness. Such things have happened to many people. Similar to this is the statement of some ignorant followers of Shaykh ‘Adī and others: “Any provision that does not come through the shaykh’s hands, I do not want.”

It is astonishing that anyone of sound mind should call upon someone who is dead and seek his aid—yet not seek the aid of the Ever-Living, who never dies!

One of them says: “If you need something from a king, you seek access to him through his aides; likewise, one seeks access to Him through the shaykhs.” These are the words of people of polytheism and misguidance. A king does not know the needs of his subjects, nor can he meet them on his own, nor does he wish to do so except for some benefit that will accrue

---

## Page 9459

to him as a result. But Allah has full knowledge of all things: He knows what is secret and what is even more hidden, and He has power over all things. All causes originate with Him and return to Him. Every cause is dependent upon other causes and subject to counteracting factors. Fire, for example, burns only what is susceptible to burning; it does not burn the salamander. When Allah wills, He prevents its effect, as He did for Abraham, peace be upon him.

The Lord’s will, however, depends on no one else, and nothing can prevent it. Whatever Allah wills happens, and whatever He does not will does not happen. He—glory be to Him—is more merciful to His servants than a mother is to her child. He treats them kindly, has mercy upon them, and relieves their suffering, although He has no need of them and they are utterly dependent upon Him.

“There is nothing like Him, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.”

The Lord thus ruled out all these possibilities, leaving only intercession. He said:

“Intercession is of no avail with Him except for those to whom He grants permission.”

And He said:

“Who is there who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

He is the One who permits intercession, and He is the One who accepts it; it all comes from Him alone. The more sincerely a person devotes himself to Allah, the more likely he is to receive the Messenger’s intercession.

Abū Hurayrah said to him: “Who will be most fortunate in receiving your intercession, O Messenger of Allah?”

He replied: “Whoever says, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship but Allah,’ seeking thereby the Face of Allah.”

As for those who place their trust in someone to intercede for them apart from Allah Most High, and attach themselves to that person, they are like the polytheists who have taken intercessors besides

---

## Page 9460

Allah Most High. Allah Most High says:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah.’”

Allah Most High also says:

“Then He rose above the Throne. You have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

And He says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove your suffering or to shift it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, vying to be nearest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

A group of the early generations said that some people used to invoke the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels. Allah Most High therefore explained that these prophets and angels are His servants, just as those who invoke them are His servants. They draw near to Allah, hope for His mercy, and fear His punishment.

The polytheists have taken rivals alongside Allah, loving them as they love Allah, and have taken intercessors to intercede for them with Allah. They thus love these beings and associate them with Allah. Their conduct resembles that of the Christians in their love for the Messiah and their association of him with Allah.

The believers, however, are stronger in their love for Allah. They worship Allah alone and do not set up anything alongside Him that they love as they love Him—neither His prophets nor anyone else. Rather, out of their love for Allah, they love what He loves. They devote their religion sincerely to Allah and know that no one can intercede for them except by His permission. Thus, they love Allah’s servant and Messenger Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, out of their love for Allah. They know that he is Allah’s servant who conveys His message, so they obey his commands and believe what he reports. They place their hope in Allah alone, fear Allah alone, and ask Allah alone. His intercession for those for whom

---

## Page 9461

he intercedes is by Allah’s permission. Neither our hope in the intercessor nor our fear of him benefits us. What benefits us is worshipping Allah alone, being sincere to Him, and placing our trust in Him, for He is the One who grants the intercessor permission. A Muslim must therefore distinguish his love and religion from those of the Christians and polytheists, follow the people of monotheism and faith, and cease resembling the polytheists and worshippers of crosses.

It is reported in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever possesses three qualities will taste the sweetness of faith: that Allah and His Messenger are dearer to him than all else; that he loves a person solely for Allah’s sake; and that he hates to return to disbelief after Allah has saved him from it as much as he hates to be thrown into the Fire.”

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your relatives, the wealth you have acquired, the trade whose decline you fear, and the homes you cherish are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command. Allah does not guide the defiantly disobedient people.’”

Allah Most High says:

“Whoever among you turns away from his religion—Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him, humble toward the believers and firm toward the disbelievers, striving in Allah’s cause and fearing no one’s reproach. That is Allah’s bounty, which He grants to whom He wills. Allah is All-Bountiful, All-Knowing.”

This is a vast subject. The religion of Islam is built upon this foundation, and the Quran revolves around it.

---

## Page 9462

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about “humility” and the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“O Allah, let me live in humility and die in humility, and gather me among the humble.”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. This hadith was narrated by al-Tirmidhī, and Abū al-Faraj mentioned it in al-Mawdū‘āt (Fabricated Reports). Whether or not its wording is authentic, the humble person who deserves praise is one who is modest and submissive before Allah. Humility does not mean lacking wealth. Indeed, a man may be poor yet tyrannical, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in an authentic hadith:

“There are three to whom Allah will not speak, nor will He look at them on the Day of Resurrection or purify them, and theirs will be a painful punishment: a lying king, an arrogant poor man, and an elderly adulterer.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to say:

“I am a servant: I eat as a servant eats and sit as a servant sits.”

Humility is therefore an inward quality: modesty, submissiveness, and gentleness—the opposite of arrogance. As Jesus, peace be upon him, said:

“And dutiful to my mother, and He has not made me a wretched tyrant.”

The poet’s words likewise illustrate this:

---

## Page 9463

“Lowly are those who love; even their graves
Bear the dust of abasement among the tombs.”

That is, they are humble and submissive, for love brings submission. Worship of Allah combines perfect love for Him with perfect submission to Him. Whoever loves something without submitting to it is not its worshipper; nor is someone who submits to it while hating it.

Love has degrees. The highest is tatayyum (enslavement by love), which is worshipful devotion. The expression tayyama Allāha means “he worshipped Allah.” Allah Most High says:

“The servants of the Most Merciful are those who walk upon the earth humbly, and when the ignorant address them, they say, ‘Peace.’”

And likewise in the verses that follow. The evidence supporting this principle is abundant.

---

## Page 9464

Shaykh al-Islām also said:

Section

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, linked restraint with self-sufficiency in several hadiths. Among them is his statement in the hadith of Abū Sa‘īd recorded in the two Sahīhs:

“Whoever seeks self-sufficiency, Allah will make him self-sufficient; and whoever seeks to refrain from begging, Allah will enable him to refrain.”

Another is his statement in the hadith of ‘Iyād ibn Himār in Sahīh Muslim:

“The people of Paradise are of three kinds: a just ruler, and a self-sufficient man who exercises restraint and gives charity.”

Another is his statement in the hadith about horses recorded in the Sahīh:

“And a man who keeps them to secure his livelihood and avoid begging, without forgetting Allah’s due concerning their necks and backs: they are a protection for him.”

Another is the report narrated from him:

“Whoever seeks wealth to be independent of others and refrain from begging will meet Allah with his face like the moon on the night when it is full.”

Another is his statement in the hadith of ‘Umar and others:

“Whatever comes to you of this wealth without your asking for it or eagerly desiring it, accept it.”

The one who asks does so with his tongue, unlike the one who refrains from begging. The one who eagerly desires does so in his heart, and this is contrary to self-sufficiency.

He said concerning the poor:

“Those unaware of their condition think them wealthy because of their restraint.”

---

## Page 9465

that is, from asking people.

He also said:

“True wealth lies not in abundant possessions, but in a self-sufficient soul.”

A self-sufficient soul does not look longingly to created beings. A free man is a slave as long as he covets, and a slave is free as long as he is content.

As it has been said:

“I obeyed my covetous desires, and they enslaved me.”

He therefore disapproved of allowing the soul to pursue whatever it eagerly desired, lest neediness and covetousness toward created beings remain in the heart. For this is contrary to the commanded reliance upon Allah and to self-sufficiency of the soul.

---

## Page 9466

Shaykh al-Islām said:

Section

It is reported in a hadith:

“The greatest major sins are disbelief and arrogance.”

This is true, for these two sins underlie every sin committed by humans and jinn. Iblīs was the first to commit them, and he is their source.

Allah Most High said:

“Except Iblīs, who refused and was arrogant, and became one of the disbelievers.”

And He said:

“Except Iblīs, who was arrogant and became one of the disbelievers.”

In Sahīh Muslim, Ibn Masʿūd reported that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“No one with an atom’s weight of faith in his heart will enter the Fire, and no one with an atom’s weight of arrogance in his heart will enter Paradise.”

He thus made arrogance the opposite of faith. The same applies to associating partners with Allah, as in His words:

“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him.”

Ibn Masʿūd said that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever dies without associating anything with Allah will enter Paradise.”

He added: “And I say: Whoever dies while associating anything with Allah will enter the Fire.”

---

## Page 9467

Some people combine both, while others exhibit only one of them. The righteous believer is one whom Allah has spared both. For a person either submits to Allah alone, submits to others alongside Him, or submits neither to Allah nor to anyone else. The first is a believer, the second one who associates partners with Allah, and the third an arrogant disbeliever—though in some cases he may not be a disbeliever. The Christians’ affliction is associating partners with Allah, while the Jews’ affliction is arrogance. As Allah Most High said concerning the Christians:

“They have taken their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah, and also the Messiah, son of Mary, though they were commanded only to worship one God. There is no deity except Him. Exalted is He above whatever they associate with Him.”

And He said concerning the Jews:

“I will turn away from My signs those who are arrogant upon the earth without right.”

For this reason, the Jews were punished by having humiliation and poverty imposed upon them, and the Christians with misguidance, religious innovations, and ignorance.

---

## Page 9468

And Shaykh al-Islām said:

Section

A further point concerning the three things that bring ruin and those that bring salvation—regarding which he mentioned that, when faced with the things that bring ruin, one should attend to one’s own affairs—is his saying:

“Avarice that is obeyed and desire that is followed.”

He thus described the former as being obeyed and the latter as being followed. This—and Allah knows best—is because desire is the soul’s love and craving for something, whether the word denotes the desire itself or the object desired.

A person governed by desire is commanded and summoned by his desire, so he follows it, just as the movements of the limbs follow the heart’s will. Hence Allah Most High said:

“Do not follow the desires of a people who went astray before and led many others astray.”

And He said:

“Who is more astray than one who follows his own desire without guidance from Allah?”

This encompasses following desires in religion, as with the Christians and those who introduce innovations concerning doctrine and divine decree. Thus the early generations used to call them “people of desires,” including the Rāfidah and the Khawārij. Such desire is found among many of the fuqarā and jurists, except those whom Allah protects.

---

## Page 9469

Our fellow scholars have differed over whether jurists who disagree fall under the designation “people of desires,” holding two views on the matter. Judge Abū Ya‘lā included them in this classification, as did Shaykh Abū Hāmid al-Isfarāyīnī, I believe, whereas Ibn ‘Aqīl rejected this.

As for “avarice that is obeyed,” we have mentioned that its harm consists in withholding good. Such withholding is not, in itself, something one loves; rather, one is driven to it by an avid attachment to whatever one is unwilling to part with. It belongs to the realm of aversion and hatred: it commands a person, and he obeys it. Not everyone who is obeyed is followed, although everyone who is followed is obeyed. A person obeys a physician, a ruler, and others in particular matters without being their follower. Following someone, however, entails obedience and more: the follower goes with him wherever he goes.

A second distinction is that what is followed is sought for its own sake: the follower’s ultimate aim is to attain and obtain it. Such is the nature of desire. What is obeyed, by contrast, serves another end. Such is the nature of avarice.

The precise meaning of avarice is an intense disposition within the soul to withhold. Thus, one may say that someone jealously guards his religion and is unwilling to relinquish it. Avarice is therefore a disposition of the soul, of which miserliness is one manifestation.

Thus, in the two Sahīh collections, Abū Hurayrah reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Beware of avarice, for avarice destroyed those before you. It commanded them to be miserly, and they were miserly; it commanded them to commit injustice, and they committed injustice; and it commanded them to sever ties of kinship, and they severed them.”

Likewise, it is reported that ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn ‘Awf used to say while circumambulating the Ka‘bah: “My Lord, protect me from

---

## Page 9470

the avarice of my own soul.”

He was told: “How often you seek refuge from that!” He replied: “If I am protected from the avarice of my own soul, I am protected from injustice, miserliness, and severing ties of kinship”—or words to that effect.

Thus, the Quran and Sunnah explain that avarice and envy belong to the same category, as in His words:

“And they feel no resentment in their hearts over what others have been given, and give them preference over themselves, even though they are in need. And whoever is protected from the avarice of his own soul—it is they who are successful.”

He describes them as giving of the good things they possess despite their own need, and as not resenting the blessings bestowed upon their brothers. The opposite of the first quality is miserliness, and the opposite of the second is envy.

Miserliness and envy therefore belong to the same category: the envious person resents what others are given, while the miser dislikes giving. Then He says:

“And whoever is protected from the avarice of his own soul—it is they who are successful.”

Avarice is the root of both miserliness and envy. It is a narrowness of soul, an unwillingness to wish good for others and an aversion to their receiving it. This gives rise to withholding benefit, which is miserliness, and harming the person who has been blessed, which is injustice. When directed toward relatives, it constitutes severing ties of kinship.

Thus, in the hadith of Abū Hurayrah narrated by . . . (1) al-Nasā’ī through Muhammad ibn ‘Ajlān, from Suhayl ibn Abī Sālih, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “There will not be together in the Fire

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) A lacuna in the original.
> These two pages contain omissions—noted by the compiler, may Allah have mercy on him—and textual corruptions at three places. The correct wording may be:

> (Thus, in the hadith of Abū Hurayrah narrated by [Ahmad and] al-Nasā’ī through Muhammad ibn ‘Ajlān, from Suhayl ibn Abī Sālih, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “A Muslim who has killed a disbeliever and thereafter follows the right course and does his best will not share the Fire with him. Dust encountered in Allah’s cause and the blazing heat of Hell will not coexist within a believer; nor will faith and envy coexist in a servant’s heart.” Al-Nasā’ī also narrated it through Jarīr, from Suhayl, [from Safwān] ibn Abī Yazīd, from al-Qa‘qā‘ ibn [al-Lajlāj], from Abū Hurayrah, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Dust encountered in Allah’s cause and the smoke of Hell will never coexist within a servant; nor will avarice and faith ever coexist in a servant’s heart.”)

---

## Page 9471

a Muslim and a disbeliever whom he killed, provided that the Muslim thereafter acts uprightly and strives to do what is right. Nor will dust in Allah’s cause and the scorching heat of Hell come together within a believer, nor faith and envy in a servant’s heart.”

Al-Nasā’ī also narrated it through Jarīr, from Suhayl, from Safwān ibn Abī Yazīd, from al-Qa‘qā‘ ibn al-Lajlāj (*), from Abū Hurayrah, who reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Dust in Allah’s cause and the smoke of Hell will never come together within a servant, nor will avarice and faith ever come together in a servant’s heart.” . . . (1)

Consider how he mentioned avarice in the well-known narrations and envy in the other—the former wording being more comprehensive—and how he linked generosity and courage in the hadith. Likewise, he said in another hadith:

“The worst qualities in a person are avarice that torments him and cowardice that robs him of his nerve.”

Thus, he praised courage in Allah’s cause and condemned avarice. Similar to this is his saying:

“There is a kind of proud bearing that Allah loves: a man’s proud bearing in battle and when giving charity.”

The point intended by citing the hadith is His statement:

“And whoever is protected from the avarice of his own soul—it is they who are successful.”

He thus confines success to those protected from the avarice of their own souls. The avaricious person is one who does not like doing good, harms himself, and resents the blessings bestowed on others.
