## Page 9472

He was asked about certain hadiths: Are they authentic? Has any recognized authority narrated them with an authentic chain of transmission? They are the following sayings attributed to the Prophet:

“When Allah first created the intellect, He said to it, ‘Come forward,’ and it came forward. Then He said to it, ‘Go back,’ and it went back. Then He said, ‘By My might and majesty, I have created nothing more honored by Me than you. Through you I take, and through you I give; through you I reward, and through you I punish.’”

And his saying:

“I have been commanded to address people according to their understanding.”

Is this the actual wording of a hadith, or has it been altered, added to, or shortened?

And his saying:

“Among the favors Allah has bestowed upon me is this: ‘I have given you the Opening of the Book. It is from the treasures of My Throne, and I have divided it into two halves between Me and you.’”

And his saying:

“People share in three things: water, pasture, and fire.”

He answered:

As for the first hadith, it is a fabricated lie according to the scholars of hadith. It is not found in any of the authoritative books of Islam. It is narrated only by the likes of Dāwūd ibn al-Muhabbir and others who have written on the intellect. It is mentioned by the authors of the Epistles of the Brethren of Purity and other such philosophical speculators. Abū Hāmid also mentioned it in some of

---

## Page 9473

his books, as did Ibn ʿArabī, Ibn Sabʿīn, and others like them. Among scholars of hadith, it is regarded as a lie attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as stated by Abū Hātim al-Rāzī, Abū al-Faraj Ibn al-Jawzī, and other authors on the science of hadith.

Nevertheless, the wording of the hadith is:

“When Allah first created the intellect, He said to it, ‘Come forward,’ and it came forward. He said to it, ‘Go back,’ and it went back. He said, ‘I have created nothing more honored by Me than you. Through you I take, and through you I give; through you I reward, and through you I punish.’”

Another version reads:

“When Allah created the intellect, He said the same to it.”

This wording means that He addressed the intellect at the very beginning of its creation. It does not state that the intellect was the first created thing. However, the philosophers who maintain the eternity of the world—the followers of Aristotle, together with those who followed their path among the esoteric Shiites, Sufis, and speculative theologians—narrated it as, “The first thing Allah created was the intellect,” with “the intellect” bearing the nominative ending -u. They did so to support their doctrine that the first thing brought into being is the First Intellect. No scholar of hadith has transmitted it with this wording. Rather, the transmitted wording, despite its weakness, indicates the opposite of this meaning, for He said:

“I have created nothing more honored by Me than you.”

This indicates that He had already created something else before it, whereas, according to the philosophers, nothing was created before what they call the First Intellect.

Furthermore, He said:

“Through you I take, and through you I give; through you I reward, and through you I punish.”

He thus made it the means for these four things, whereas according to those esotericist philosophers:

---

## Page 9474

the entire universe emanated from the First Intellect, which, according to them, is the lord of the heavens, the earth, and everything between them, although it is itself subject to the lordship of the One whose existence is necessary in Himself. They hold that it is generated from Allah and is a necessary concomitant of His essence. This is not the view of any adherent of the religious communities—Muslims, Jews, or Christians—except those among them who have deviated from their faith. Nor is it the view of the Magians, the majority of the Sabians, most polytheists, or the majority of philosophers. Rather, it is the view of one group among the philosophers.

Furthermore, in Muslim usage, the intellect is an accident inhering in something else: an innate faculty, knowledge, or action based on knowledge. In their usage, the intellect is not a self-subsisting substance. The first created thing therefore cannot be an accident inhering in something else, for an accident can exist only in a substrate; it cannot exist before any concrete entity exists.

Those philosophers, however, define it in their terminology as a self-subsisting substance. This is not what “intellect” means in Muslim usage. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, addressed the Muslims in the language of the Arabs, not that of the Greeks. It is therefore clear that, even if the Messenger had uttered this wording, he would not have intended the meaning meant by these philosophers. How much more so, then, when he did not utter it!

As for the second hadith, namely the saying:

“I have been commanded to address people according to their level of understanding,”

this has not been narrated by any Muslim scholar whose transmission is relied upon, nor is it found in any of their books. The speech with which Allah and His Messenger address people

---

## Page 9475

is general in scope and encompasses all those subject to religious obligations, as in His words:

“O mankind!”

“O you who believe!”

“O My servants!”

“O Children of Israel!”

Likewise, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would address the people from his pulpit with the same words for everyone to hear. Yet people differ in their understanding of those words according to the strength of understanding and soundness of belief that Allah grants each of them.

For this reason, Abū Bakr al-Siddīq understood his intent better than anyone else, as related in the two Sahīh collections on the authority of Abū Sa'īd:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, addressed the people, saying: ‘Allah gave a servant a choice between this world and the Hereafter, and that servant chose what is with Allah.’ Abū Bakr wept and said: ‘We would ransom you with our lives and our wealth!’ The people were astonished at him and said: ‘How astonishing this old man is! He weeps because the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned a servant whom Allah had given a choice between this world and the Hereafter.’ It was the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who had been given the choice, and Abū Bakr understood him better than any of us.”

Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned an unspecified servant without identifying him, but Abū Bakr knew exactly who he was.

As for what some people relate from 'Umar, that he said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and Abū Bakr would converse, while I was like a man of the Zanj between them,”

this is a fabricated lie. Likewise, the report that he gave Abū Bakr one answer and 'Ā'ishah another is a lie, by the agreement of the scholars.

---

## Page 9476

He was asked about the following hadiths:

“Whoever circumambulates this House seven times, with faith and seeking Allah’s reward, will have his past sins forgiven.”

And the saying attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever stands at ‘Arafāt and thinks that Allah will not forgive him—may Allah not forgive him.”

And also:

“If a shepherd were to pass through ‘Arafāt—without knowing that it was the Day of ‘Arafah—he would be forgiven.”

And the saying attributed to him, peace be upon him:

“Whoever performs Hajj but does not visit me has slighted me, and whoever visits me will be entitled to my intercession.”

Are these hadiths found in the Sahīh collections or not? And what is the meaning of His words, Mighty and Majestic:

“The standing place of Abraham; whoever enters it shall be safe”?

He answered:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. None of these hadiths appears in either the Sahīh or the Sunan collections. Some of them convey meanings contrary to the Quran and Sunnah. If a man were to stand at ‘Arafāt fearing that Allah might not forgive his sins because they are major sins, it could not be said that Allah would not forgive him. Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives whatever is less than that for whomever He wills. Thus, He may forgive a person’s sins that fall short of associating partners with Him if He wills, or He may withhold forgiveness if He wills. However, when a servant repents of a sin, Allah forgives it, whether it involves associating partners with Him or anything else.

As Allah Most High says:

“O My servants who have transgressed against

---

## Page 9477

themselves, do not despair of Allah’s mercy. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins.” This applies to the one who repents.

Moreover, standing at ‘Arafāt does not discharge a person’s obligations of prayer and zakat, by the consensus of the Muslims. Indeed, they agree that prayer is incomparably more binding than Hajj. Hajj is obligatory once in a lifetime for those able to undertake it, and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, performed Hajj only once after the Hijrah. Prayer, by contrast, is obligatory upon every sane adult—except menstruating women and women experiencing postnatal bleeding—whether healthy or ill, safe or fearful, rich or poor, male or female. Over each day and night, there are some forty units of prayer: seventeen obligatory units, ten or twelve units of regular Sunnah prayers, and eleven or thirteen units of night prayer.

Likewise, people’s rights arising from sins, injustices and other matters are not discharged through Hajj, by the agreement of the imams. The report narrated from ‘Abbās ibn Mirdās concerning the discharge of injustices and other liabilities through Hajj is weak.

It is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The five daily prayers, from one Friday prayer to the next, and from one Ramadan to the next, expiate the sins committed between them, provided that major sins are avoided.”

These observances are greater than Hajj. Major sins, however, are expiated by repentance from them, according to the Quran, the Sunnah and the consensus of the Muslim community.

---

## Page 9478

Likewise, the saying attributed to him,

“Whoever performs Hajj but does not visit me has slighted me,”

is a fabrication. Slighting the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is forbidden, whereas visiting his grave is not obligatory, by the agreement of the Muslims. No authentic hadith from him concerning visiting his grave has been established. Indeed, reports such as “Whoever visits me and my father in a single year, I guarantee him Paradise from Allah,” and others like it, are fabrications by the agreement of the scholars. Al-Dāraqutnī and others narrated hadiths concerning visiting his grave, but they are weak.

Imam Mālik—who was among those most knowledgeable about the rights of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the Sunnah followed by the inhabitants of his city among the Companions, the Successors and their successors—disapproved of saying, “I visited the grave of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.” Had this wording been authentically established from Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and known to the scholars of Madinah, Mālik would not have disapproved of it.

As for saying, “I greeted Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,” this is unobjectionable by agreement. As reported from him in the Sunan collections, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No man greets me with peace but Allah restores my soul to me so that I may return his greeting.”

Ibn ‘Umar used to say: “Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allah. Peace be upon you, O Abū Bakr. Peace be upon you, my father.”

In Sunan Abī Dāwūd, he is reported to have said:

“Send abundant blessings upon me on Friday and the night before it, for your blessings are presented to me.” They asked, “How will our blessings be presented to you when you have decayed?” He replied, “Allah has indeed forbidden

---

## Page 9479

the earth to consume the flesh of the prophets.”

As for His words, Exalted is He,

“Whoever enters it is safe,”

this concerns the Sacred House. As He, Exalted is He, says:

“Have they not seen that We have made a secure sanctuary, while people are snatched away all around them?”

And He, Exalted is He, says:

“Let them worship the Lord of this House, who has fed them against hunger and secured them against fear.”

And He, Exalted is He, says:

“Have We not established for them a secure sanctuary to which fruits of every kind are brought?”

During the days of pre-Islamic ignorance, they would kill one another outside the Sanctuary. But when they entered the Sanctuary, even a man who encountered his father’s killer would leave him unharmed. This was among the signs Allah placed within it, as He says:

“In it are clear signs, including the standing place of Abraham. Whoever enters it is safe.”

Islam further increased its sanctity. Thus, the position of most jurists is that someone who commits an offence punishable by a prescribed penalty outside the Sanctuary and then takes refuge within it is not subjected to that penalty until he leaves. This was the view expressed by Ibn ‘Umar and Ibn ‘Abbās, and it is the position of Abū Hanīfah, Ahmad and others. For it is authentically established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah made Makkah sacred; it was not people who made it sacred. It is therefore not lawful for anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day to shed blood or cut down trees there. Its inviolability was not suspended for anyone before me, nor will it be suspended for anyone after me. It was suspended for me only for an hour of one day, and today its sanctity has returned to what it was yesterday.”

---

## Page 9480

Whoever thinks that a person who enters the Sanctuary is safe from punishment in the Hereafter despite neglecting prayer and other obligatory duties and committing forbidden acts has contradicted the consensus of the Muslims. Indeed, among those who have entered the House are disbelievers, hypocrites and sinners who, by the consensus of the Muslims, are among the people of Hell. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9481

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about the following hadith:

“If someone teaches you a verse from the Book of Allah, it is as though he owns you as a slave: he may sell you if he wishes or set you free if he wishes.”

Is this found in the Six Books, or is it a lie attributed to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?

He replied:

This is not found in any of the Muslims’ books, neither the Six Books nor any others. Rather, it contradicts the consensus of the Muslims. Teaching someone does not make the teacher that person’s owner, entitled to sell him or set him free as he wishes. Anyone who believes this is to be called upon to repent; if he repents, well and good; otherwise, he is to be put to death. A free Muslim cannot be enslaved.

The foremost teacher of mankind, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taught them the Book and wisdom, and he has a greater claim over them than they have over themselves. Nevertheless, they remained free: he neither enslaved them nor made them his servants in bondage. Rather, the rulings governing his relations with the free members of his community differed from those governing his relations with the slaves he owned. Had the believing women been his property, he would have been permitted to have intercourse with any believing woman without a marriage contract. Likewise, anyone who taught a woman a verse of the Quran would have been entitled to have intercourse with her without marriage. No Muslim would say this.

---

## Page 9482

He was asked about the meaning of the Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Whoever rebukes an adherent of religious innovation, Allah will fill his heart with security and faith and grant him safety on the Day of the Greatest Terror.”

He replied:

As for the sayings, “Whoever rebukes an adherent of religious innovation, Allah will fill his heart with security and faith,” and “Whoever honors an adherent of religious innovation helps to destroy Islam,” and similar statements, these words are known to have been said by al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād.

Religious innovation consists of beliefs and acts of worship that contradict the Book and the Sunnah or the consensus of the early generations of the Muslim community. Examples include the doctrines of the Khawārij, Rāfidah, Qadariyyah and Jahmiyyah; the practices of those who worship through dancing and singing in mosques, or through shaving their beards and consuming hashish; and similar innovations adopted as acts of worship by groups that oppose the Book and the Sunnah. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9483

He was asked about someone who heard a man say:

“If you had done such and such, none of this would have happened to you.”

Another man who heard him said, “The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade this expression. It is an expression that leads the one who utters it to disbelief.”

A third man said, “Regarding the story of Moses and al-Khidr, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

‘May Allah have mercy on Moses. We wish he had been patient, so that Allah might have told us more of their story.’”

The other man cited the Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak believer”—until he said—“for the word ‘if’ opens the way for Satan’s work.”

Does one of these abrogate the other, or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Everything Allah and His Messenger have said is true. The word “if” is used in two ways:

(The first is to express grief over the past and distress at what has been decreed. This is what he forbade, as Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, do not be

---

## Page 9484

like those who disbelieved and said of their brothers who travelled through the land or went out to battle, ‘Had they stayed with us, they would not have died or been killed,’ so that Allah may make this a source of regret in their hearts.” This is what the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade when he said:

“If something befalls you, do not say, ‘If only I had done such and such, this and that would have happened.’ Rather, say, ‘Allah decreed it, and He does whatever He wills,’ for ‘if’ opens the way for Satan’s work.”

That is, it opens the way for grief and distress, which harm you and bring no benefit. Rather, know that whatever befell you could never have missed you, and whatever missed you could never have befallen you. As Allah Most High says:

“No calamity befalls except by Allah’s permission. And whoever believes in Allah, He guides his heart.”

They explained: this is the man who suffers a calamity and knows that it is from Allah, so he is content and submits.

(The second use of “if” is to convey beneficial knowledge, as in the words of Allah Most High:

“Had there been gods besides Allah in the heavens and the earth, both would have fallen into ruin.”

It may also express love of good and the desire to do it, as in the saying, “If I had wealth like so-and-so, I would do as he does.” This and similar expressions are permissible. The Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“I wish Moses had been patient, so that Allah might have told us more of their story,”

belongs to this category, as does His saying:

“They wish you would compromise, so that they too would compromise.”

Our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, wished that Allah would recount their story. He therefore mentioned them both to express his love for the patience on which that depended, thereby making known what would have happened, because of the benefit in that. This involved neither distress nor grief, nor any abandonment of what

---

## Page 9485

he loved: patience in bearing what had been decreed.

Regarding his words:

“I wish Moses had been patient,”

the grammarians explain the underlying construction as: “I wish that Moses had been patient.” Likewise, regarding His words:

“They wish you would compromise, so that they too would compromise,”

the underlying construction is: “They wish that you would compromise.” Some, however, say that “if” is a conditional particle here, with its consequent omitted. The meaning is clear on either analysis: loving and desiring that action. Loving and desiring good is praiseworthy, whereas grief, distress, and abandoning patience are blameworthy. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9486

He was asked:

“Regarding the account of Iblīs speaking to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, while he was in the mosque with a group of his Companions, and the Prophet questioning him about many matters while the people plainly saw his form and heard him speaking aloud: is this an authentic hadith or a fabricated lie? Does it appear in any of the Sahīh, Musnad, or Sunan collections? Is it permissible for anyone to narrate it? What should be done about someone who narrates it to people and claims that it is authentic and religiously authoritative?”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Rather, this is a fabricated lie. It is not found in any of the Muslims’ authoritative hadith collections—neither the Sahīh collections, nor the Sunan collections, nor the Musnad collections.

Anyone who knows that it is a lie attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is not permitted to narrate it from him. Anyone who claims that it is authentic is to be informed of its true status; if he persists, he is to be punished for that.

However, it contains much material compiled from prophetic hadiths. The person who fabricated and invented it assembled it from reports, some false and some true. This is why it contains several authentic statements, even though the underlying account—Iblīs appearing visibly before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the presence of his Companions, and the Prophet questioning him—is a fabricated lie that none of the Muslim scholars has transmitted. And Allah, glorified and exalted is He, knows best.

---

## Page 9487

He also said—may Allah Most High have mercy on him:

The book Tanaqqulāt al-Anwār (The Passage of the Lights), attributed to Ahmad ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Bakrī, is among the books most filled with lies and fabrications against Allah, His Messenger, and the Companions of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Its author has invented accounts like those fabricated in the tales of Dalhama and al-Battāl, the tales of ‘Antara, the stories of al-Rashīd and his vizier Ja‘far al-Barmakī, and the stories of adventurers such as al-Zi’baq the Egyptian and Ahmad al-Danq, and other such works.

But those writers fabricate lies about people who are not prophets, whereas the author of the book he called Tanaqqulāt al-Anwār fabricates lies about Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions. He attributes to him lies the like of which no one is known to have written in any book. Even if some of what he relates contains a very little truth, it is of the same kind as that found in the tales of ‘Antara and al-Battāl. ‘Antara was indeed a poet and one of the horsemen of the pre-Islamic era. His poetry is well known, and one of his odes is among the seven Mu‘allaqāt (Suspended Odes). Yet they fabricated more lies about him than anyone but Allah can count, and each successive storyteller added further falsehoods to those tales.

---

## Page 9488

Likewise, Abū Muhammad al-Battāl was a well-known Muslim commander. The Muslims had undertaken two expeditions against Constantinople.

The first was during the caliphate of Mu‘āwiya, who placed his son Yazīd in command. Abū Ayyūb al-Ansārī accompanied him—the Companion in whose house the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stayed when he emigrated to Medina. Abū Ayyūb died during that expedition and was buried near Constantinople. Al-Bukhārī narrated in his Sahīh, from Ibn ‘Umar, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The first army to campaign against Constantinople will be forgiven.”

The second expedition was during the caliphate of ‘Abd al-Malik ibn Marwān. He placed his son Maslama in command—or left his son al-Walīd in his place—and sent a vast army with him. They besieged the city and remained there for several years. They then concluded a peace agreement with its people allowing them to enter it, and built a mosque there. That mosque remains to this day.

Then the liars came and added to the tales of al-Battāl and ‘Abd al-Wahhāb more falsehoods than anyone but Allah can count. These accounts mentioned Dalhama, the judge ‘Aqibah, and things with no basis in reality.

Al-Bakrī, the author of Tanaqqulāt al-Anwār (The Passage of the Lights), followed the path of these lying fabricators. Yet his lies about Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions—the finest of creation after the prophets—are even more numerous. His book contains kinds of fabricated falsehoods and outlandish inventions that defy description, such as the tale of the Seven Fortresses

---

## Page 9489

and Haddām ibn Jihāf, and tales such as Time, The Ghoul’s Head, Kalindaja, and other works of his. These mention places that do not exist, campaigns that never occurred, and names and entities unknown to any scholar. They transmit reports that contradict Allah’s Book, His Messenger’s Sunnah, the consensus of the Muslims, and what has been transmitted through numerous independent chains from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

They attribute to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions words and deeds of which Allah has declared him innocent. These resemble the reports of the Nusayrī heretics and their like, who fabricate accounts involving excessive veneration of ‘Alī and others. They contain attacks upon Islam and corruption of it that warrant putting those who utter such things to death. If such a person is ignorant, he must be called upon to repent; if he repents, he is spared, but otherwise he is executed. The least that should be done to anyone who narrates such material is to punish him sufficiently to deter him from doing so again. Likewise, anyone who rents these books to someone who reads them and believes their contents deserves punishment, as does anyone who copies them.

Scholars must expose the falsehood they recognize in these works and others like them. For just as it is obligatory to expose the falsehood of what has been transmitted from him in hadiths such as those of al-Bukhārī, it is obligatory to expose the falsehood of the fabricated hadiths falsely attributed to him that are known to be lies, just as scholars have explained the circumstances of those narrators who lied about him

---

## Page 9490

and exposed the false reports attributed to him that they know to be lies. Many fabricated reports are recognized as such only by specialists in hadith. As for reports such as those found in *Tanaqqulāt al-Anwār*, anyone with even the slightest knowledge of the Messenger’s life and military campaigns knows that they are false.

The authorities must punish anyone who narrates these reports or assists in doing so in any way. The ruler may burn these books, for ‘Uthmān, may Allah be pleased with him, burned books, and these are more deserving of being burned than those were. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9491

What do the esteemed scholars—may Allah be pleased with them all—say concerning storytellers who recount the Prophet’s military campaigns, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the stories of the prophets, peace be upon them, below the citadel, in congregational mosques and in marketplaces? They say:

An angel named Habīb came to the Prophet and said to him, “If you are Allah’s Messenger, we want the moon, on the twenty-ninth night, to return, descend through your collar and emerge from your sleeves.” He showed them this, and they all believed in him. He said, “They were the Lord.”

They also say that a king named Bashīr ibn Ghannām came to him, tricked him and seized nine people from him, whom he hung on palm trees. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, sent ‘Alī, who rescued them. Khālid was among them.

A king called al-Dihāq came to him while he was in Mecca. He had a daughter named Hamānah. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, then broke [him] and married his daughter to Bilāl. He then killed him while he was praying. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, laid down his cloak, and Allah restored him to life for him.

---

## Page 9492

and that al-Miqdād was sent to a king called al-Malik al-Khattār. On his way, he met a queen named Rawdah and married her. He then went to the king to whom he had been sent, fought him, took him captive and came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Būlus ibn ‘Abd al-Salīb fought in the expedition of Tabūk.

And that he fought at the Battle of the Confederates, when they numbered thousands. The Confederates broke before ‘Alī into seventeen divisions, behind each of which stood a man striking with a sword and saying, “I am ‘Alī—his ally.” He struck ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āmirī and severed his thigh. ‘Amr took his thigh and struck the Muslims with it, then uprooted a tree and killed a number of them with it. At that, the angels cried out, saying:

“There is no sword but Dhū al-Fiqār,
and no valiant warrior but ‘Alī.”

And that ‘Alī fought the jinn in the well and was launched by catapult towards the fortress of al-Ghurāb. His flight fell short, so he walked through the air. And that he struck Marhab the Jew, who had a marble basin on his head, splitting both him and his horse in two. And that the army crossed over his forearm to Khaybar, and he demolished the fortress. And that Dhū al-Fiqār was sent down to him from heaven, for Allah named him from heaven and said, “‘Alī is swifter than haste.”

And that he was sent secretly with every prophet, but openly with the Prophet; that he was Moses’ staff, Noah’s ark and Solomon’s ring; and that he drank from the Prophet’s navel, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, when he died, and weighed the knowledge of the first and the last.

And that the Angel of Death came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the guise of a Bedouin

---

## Page 9493

and the Prophet said to him, “Have you come to take my soul or to visit?” He replied, “I have never visited anyone before you, that I should visit you.” He then gave him an apple, which he smelled, and his soul departed into it. And that Fātimah wept over him until she disturbed the people of Medina, who took her out to the houses of mourning. They also recount stories about the prophets like those mentioned in this question, explaining them through verses never heard from the scholars. They gather together for a night over each of these accounts.

Some scholars had forbidden them to transmit such material, requiring them to transmit only material bearing records of readings before learned shaykhs. They therefore relied on books containing accounts like those mentioned above, composed by a man called al-Bakrī. What, then, must they do concerning such matters? For they transmit things that contradict what is reliably reported from the messengers, peace be upon them, and some of what they transmit disparages them. Will someone who orders that they be stopped be rewarded?

They also relate that Allah took a handful of the light of His Face and looked at it, whereupon it sweated and shed drops. Allah created a prophet from every drop. That handful was the Prophet, and a brilliant star remained; it was a light passed from the loins of men to the wombs of women.

Shaykh al-Islām, the exemplar of faith, Taqī al-Dīn Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Halīm ibn ‘Abd al-Salām Ibn Taymiyyah al-Harrānī, answered, saying:

---

## Page 9494

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. These reports are fabrications by the consensus of the scholars. Such reports are drawn only from works like al-Bakrī’s Tanaqqulāt al-anwār (The Passage of the Lights), and from others like him who transmitted numerous lies.

As for the first account, the moon did not enter the neck opening of the Prophet’s clothing, peace and blessings be upon him, nor his garments, nor did it touch him. Rather, it split into two parts: one below the mountain and the other above it. Likewise, Habīb Abī Mālik never existed. The report mentioned about Bashīr ibn Ghannām is also a lie, and this name is unknown. Khālid ibn al-Walīd was never taken captive at all. Rather, he embraced Islam after al-Hudaybiyyah and remained victorious in his battles.

Likewise, what is related about the so-called King al-Dihāq is a lie. No one bearing this name was among those whom the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, fought. [He] lived; however, among those in this community who lived after death, there was a group in the time of the Companions and their Successors. As for the believers whose mounts Allah restored to life after death, some were Muslims during the lifetime of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, while others lived after his death, peace and blessings be upon him.

---

## Page 9495

Likewise, what is related about the king named al-Khitār is a fabrication; no such person existed. As for the expedition to Tabūk, no fighting took place there. Rather, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, advanced towards the Byzantines, Arabs, and others in Syria. Never did more Muslims gather for an expedition with the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, than gathered with him in the year of Tabūk, which was his last military expedition. He stayed at Tabūk for twenty days, but the Christians did not come out to confront him.

Likewise, during the campaign of the Confederates, there was no battle between the two armies. Rather, the Confederates besieged the Muslims from outside the trench that the Muslims had dug around Medina, while the Muslims remained inside it. There were a few skirmishes between some Muslims and some disbelievers, amounting to single combat or something similar. ʿAlī—may Allah be pleased with him—killed ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Wudd al-ʿĀmirī. The Confederates were not defeated through combat, nor were any significant numbers killed on either side. Rather, Allah sent against them the wind—the east wind—and sent the angels, as the Most High says in the account of the Confederates:

“O you who believe, remember Allah’s favor upon you when armies came against you, and We sent against them a wind and forces you did not see.”

And the following verses.

The account given of how ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Wudd al-ʿĀmirī was killed is a lie. Likewise, the claim that ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Wudd struck a tree with his thigh and uprooted it is a lie. There were no trees there; the date palms were far from the encampment.

Likewise, the account of a caller proclaiming:

“There is no sword but Dhū

---

## Page 9496

al-Fiqār, and no hero but ʿAlī,” is a fabricated lie. Anyone who reports that this occurred on the day of Badr or on some other occasion is likewise lying. Dhū al-Fiqār was not ʿAlī’s sword; rather, it belonged to Abū Jahl, from whom the Muslims captured it as booty on the day of Badr. It was a sword made of metal. No sword descended from heaven, nor did this sword—or any other—grow longer.

Likewise, what he mentions about fighting the jinn—that ʿAlī or another human fought them in the well of Dhāt al-ʿIlm, or that some other human did so—is all lies. The jinn would never have fought the Companions in the first place. Rather, the disbelieving jinn used to fight the believing jinn. As for ʿAlī and his fellow Companions, their standing was far too great for the jinn to stand their ground against them in battle. It is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb:

“Satan never sees you taking a path without taking a path other than yours.”

The account of ʿAlī being launched from a catapult and of the siege of the fortress called the Raven’s Fort is entirely a fabricated lie. The Muslims never launched anyone from a catapult towards the disbelievers—neither ʿAlī nor anyone else. Indeed, during the lifetime of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, the Muslims only ever set up a catapult against al-Tā’if, when the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, besieged it after the Battle of Hunayn and the defeat of Hawāzin. He besieged al-Tā’if, set up the catapult, and remained there for a month. It was not conquered until its people later embraced Islam voluntarily. And when

---

## Page 9497

the Muslims were fighting Musaylimah the Liar and his followers, they drove them into their garden. The men then lifted al-Barā’ ibn Mālik and threw him over the wall into their midst, whereupon he opened the gate for them.

As for the account of Marhab, it is reported in the Sahīh that ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, killed Marhab. It is also reported in the Sahīh that Muhammad ibn Maslamah killed Marhab. Some have said, “Rather, one of the two reports is mistaken.” As for the claim that the helmet on his head was a marble basin, that is a lie. Likewise, the claim that the blow split both the horseman and his horse and continued down into the ground—all of this is false. Scholars of the military campaigns and the Prophet’s biography have transmitted nothing of this kind; only ignorant people and liars relate it.

Even more obviously false is the account of the army crossing over ‘Alī’s forearm, the mule passing over it, and ‘Alī praying that it should have no offspring. Such stories are related only by people who are among the most ignorant of the Companions’ circumstances and of the realities of the world. Mules have always been sterile. There was no mule whatsoever in the army at Khaybar. Nor was there a mule with the Muslims, in Medina, or in the surrounding lands of Arabia, except the mule that al-Muqawqis, the ruler of Egypt, presented to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. He presented it to him after Khaybar; for when the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, concluded a peace agreement with the people of al-Hudaybiyah, he set out on his return journey

---

## Page 9498

and Allah enabled them to conquer Khaybar. He then returned and sent his envoys to the rulers: Chosroes, Caesar, al-Muqawqis, and the Arab rulers in Syria, Yemen, al-Yamāmah, and the East. What is known among the scholars, however, is that ‘Alī tore out the gate of Khaybar.

The claim that Dhū al-Fiqār descended from heaven is a lie. As explained earlier, it was one of Abū Jahl’s swords, which the Muslims took from him as spoils on the day of Badr. As for ‘Alī, his father gave him this name before Allah sent Muhammad as a prophet and before anyone, whether man or boy, had acquired the status of a Muslim.

As for the claim that “he was Moses’ staff, Noah’s ark, and Solomon’s ring,” no rational person who understands what he is saying would utter this. It resembles the speech of madmen more than that of rational people. Only someone profoundly ignorant would intend this as praise of ‘Alī. For ‘Alī, and even those Companions below him in rank, are more honourable in Allah’s sight than these inanimate objects. Although the staff was a sign granted to Moses, not everything that served as a miracle for a prophet is superior to the believers. Rather, the believers are superior to the birds that the Messiah would fashion from clay and breathe into, so that they became birds by Allah’s permission. They are also superior to the locusts, lice, frogs, and blood that served as signs for Moses; superior to the staff and the serpent; and superior to Sālih’s she-camel. Whoever thinks that he is praising ‘Alī through such lies and ignorance is as ignorant of what constitutes praise and commendation as he is of the fact that these inanimate objects were never human beings.

---

## Page 9499

As for the claim that he drank from the navel of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and thereby acquired the knowledge of the first and the last generations, this too is a lie. The knowledge that ‘Alī learned from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had already been acquired before the Prophet’s death. Whatever understanding, learning through hearing, and additional knowledge Allah granted him after the Prophet’s death did not come from drinking water from his navel. Nor has anyone ever acquired any knowledge at all by drinking from a prophet or anyone else. None of the Companions—neither Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, ‘Uthmān, ‘Alī, nor anyone else—possessed the knowledge of the first and the last generations.

The virtues of the Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, established in the authentic hadith collections are such that Allah has left no need for the lies of fabricators. These include the Prophet’s statement, authentically transmitted through several chains:

“Were I to take anyone on earth as an intimate friend, I would take Abū Bakr as an intimate friend.”

And his statement:

“Let every small doorway into the mosque be sealed except that of Abū Bakr.”

And his statement:

“No one has done more for us through his companionship and wealth than Abū Bakr.”

And his statement:

“O people, I came to you and said, ‘I am Allah’s Messenger to you.’ You said, ‘You are lying,’ but Abū Bakr said, ‘You speak the truth.’ Will you not leave my companion alone for my sake? Will you not leave my companion alone for my sake? Will you not leave my companion alone for my sake?”

And his repeatedly issued instruction during the illness in which he died:

“Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”

Another example is his statement to ‘Ā’ishah: “Call your father and your brother to me so that I may write a document for Abū Bakr, so that people will not disagree after me.” Then he said: “Allah will not accept

---

## Page 9500

—nor will the believers—anyone but Abū Bakr.” And there are similar reports.

Another example is his statement:

“Among the nations before you were men granted inspiration. If there is anyone like that in my community, it is ‘Umar.”

And his statement to ‘Umar:

“Whenever Satan sees you taking a path, he takes a different path.”

And his statement:

“I saw in a dream that a vessel of milk was brought to me. I drank from it, then handed what remained to ‘Umar.” They asked, “How did you interpret it?” He replied, “Knowledge.”

And his statement:

“I saw in a dream that people were being presented before me wearing shirts. Some reached their chests, while others were shorter. ‘Umar was presented before me wearing a shirt so long that he was dragging it.” They asked, “How did you interpret it?” He replied, “Religion.”

And his statement:

“I saw in a dream that I was drawing water from a well. Then Ibn Abī Quhāfah took the bucket and drew one or two bucketfuls, though there was weakness in his drawing—may Allah forgive him. Then Ibn al-Khattāb took it, and it became a great bucket. I have never seen a man of exceptional strength draw water as he did, until the people departed with their camels fully watered.”

There are similar reports, such as his statement about ‘Uthmān:

“Should I not feel shy before a man before whom the angels of heaven feel shy?”

And the report:

“He said, ‘Who will buy the well of Rūmah and have Paradise?’ So ‘Uthmān bought it.”

And his statement about ‘Uthmān when he equipped the Army of Hardship:

“Whatever ‘Uthmān does after today will not harm him.”

And his statement on the day of the Pledge of Divine Pleasure, when he received the Muslims’ pledge beneath the tree:

“This hand of mine represents ‘Uthmān’s right hand.”

He had sent ‘Uthmān as an emissary to the people of Mecca. Ibn ‘Umar said: “During the lifetime of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, we used to say: ‘Abū Bakr, then ‘Umar, then ‘Uthmān.’” And there are similar reports.

---

## Page 9501

Another example is his statement in the year of Khaybar:

“Tomorrow I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger, and whom Allah and His Messenger love. Allah will grant victory through him.”

‘Alī had remained behind in Medina because he was suffering from sore eyes. He then caught up with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. When morning came, ‘Alī arrived, and he gave him the banner; eventually Allah granted victory through him.

“When he set out on the expedition to Tabūk with all the people, allowing only those with valid excuses to remain behind, he appointed ‘Alī to oversee Medina in his absence. Some hypocrites disparaged ‘Alī, so he caught up with him, weeping, and said, ‘Are you leaving me behind with the women and children?’ He replied, ‘Are you not content to stand in relation to me as Aaron stood in relation to Moses, except that there is no prophet after me?’ He also wrapped his cloak around ‘Alī, Fātimah, Hasan and Husayn, saying, ‘O Allah, these are the members of my household. Remove impurity from them and purify them completely.’”

“When he intended to engage in a mutual invocation of Allah’s curse with the people of Najrān, he took ‘Alī, Fātimah, Hasan and Husayn and went out with them to do so.”

“When ‘Alī, Ja‘far and Zayd disputed over the custody of Hamzah’s daughter, he awarded custody to her maternal aunt, who was married to Ja‘far. He said to Ja‘far, ‘You resemble me in appearance and character.’ He said to ‘Alī, ‘You are of me, and I am of you.’ And he said to Zayd, ‘You are our brother and our freedman.’”

He likewise said:

“When the people of the Ash‘arī tribe run short of provisions while travelling, or their households in Medina have little food, they gather whatever they have in a single cloth and then divide it equally among themselves. They are of me, and I am of them.”

And he said:

“Every community has a trusted man, and the trusted man of this community is Abū ‘Ubaydah ibn al-Jarrāh.”

---

## Page 9502

And he said:

“Every prophet has devoted supporters, and my devoted supporter is al-Zubayr.”

These hadiths and others like them in the authentic collections leave no need for fabrications. Likewise, the account of the Angel of Death coming in the form of a Bedouin and giving the Prophet an apple, which he smelled, is also a fabrication. Indeed, the lengthy report concerning the Prophet’s death, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—which relates that the Angel of Death knocked at the door, that people came out to him one after another, and that, upon recognizing him, they humbled themselves before him—is also a fabrication, by agreement of the scholars versed in hadith. This is so despite its having been transmitted by al-Tabarānī through ‘Abd al-Mun‘im ibn Idrīs, from his father, from Wahb ibn Munabbih, from Ibn ‘Abbās. This ‘Abd al-Mun‘im is notorious for fabrications.

Likewise, the account that Fātimah wept over the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, until she disturbed the people of Medina and they sent her out to the houses of mourning is another invented falsehood. Only an ignorant person, or someone intent on reviling Fātimah and the Companions—may Allah be pleased with them—would transmit such a story, attributing to them conduct from which Allah has preserved Fātimah and the Companions.

Likewise, the claim that “Allah took a handful of the light of His Face and looked at it, whereupon it sweated and overflowed; then He created a prophet from every drop, and that the handful was

---

## Page 9503

the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and that he remained a radiant star”—this too is a lie, by agreement of those versed in his hadiths.

The same applies to similar reports, such as those mentioned by Shīrawayh al-Daylamī in his book al-Firdaws (Paradise), and by Ibn Hamawayh in his writings on realities, such as al-Mahbūb (The Beloved). These include claims that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was a star; that the entire world was created from him; that he existed before his parents were created; or that he had memorized the Quran before Gabriel brought it to him. All these claims and others like them are fabricated lies, by agreement of those knowledgeable about his life.

None of the prophets was created from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Rather, each was created from his own parents, and Allah breathed the spirit into him. Nor was it the case that whenever Allah taught His messengers and prophets through revelation, they received it through an intermediary other than Gabriel. Rather, sometimes Allah speaks to them through revelation that He imparts to them; sometimes He speaks to them from behind a veil, as He spoke to Moses son of ‘Imrān; and sometimes He sends an angel who reveals, by His permission, whatever He wills.

Some prophets follow the revealed law of another prophet, just as the prophets of the Children of Israel followed the law of the Torah. As for the claim that they all receive from a single individual, this and similar claims are made by the people of

---

## Page 9504

heresy among the adherents of monism and unionism, such as Ibn ‘Arabī, author of al-Futūhāt al-Makkiyya (The Meccan Revelations), al-Fusūs (The Bezels), and similar works. For when his doctrine was set out—namely, that existence is one and that the Creator’s existence is the very existence of created things, even though the fixed entities in nonexistence are many—he said:

“This knowledge belongs only to the Seal of the Messengers and the Seal of the Saints. No prophet or messenger perceives it except through the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Messengers, and no saint perceives it except through the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Saints. Even the messengers, when they perceive it, perceive it only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Saints. For messengership and prophethood—by which I mean lawgiving prophethood and messengership—come to an end, whereas sainthood never comes to an end. Thus, the messengers, in their capacity as saints, perceive it only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Saints.”

He continued until he stated that the Seal of the Prophets occupies the place of a silver brick, whereas the Seal of the Saints occupies the place of two bricks: one gold and one silver. He thus occupies the place of the silver brick, which represents his outward aspect and the associated legal rulings. Since he sees the matter as it truly is, he must see it in this way. Inwardly, he occupies the place of the gold brick, for he draws from the same source as the angel who brings revelation to the messengers.

These and similar words contain much misguidance, such as his claim that all the prophets and messengers derive their knowledge of Allah from the Seal of the Prophets. This is a lie.

---

## Page 9505

And whoever says that Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, Moses, Jesus, and the others derived their knowledge of Allah only from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has spoken falsely. Rather, Allah revealed to them and taught them. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not yet exist when they were created, and those who come earlier do not derive their knowledge from those who come later.

His statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “I was a prophet while Adam was between spirit and body”—or, in another wording, “I was recorded as a prophet”—is like his statement:

“I am indeed recorded with Allah as the Seal of the Prophets while Adam still lay in his clay form.”

For Allah created Adam’s body and, before breathing the spirit into him, recorded and made known what would happen among his descendants. Thus, He recorded Muhammad’s prophethood and made it known. Likewise, it is established in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The creation of each of you is gathered in his mother’s womb for forty days as a drop, then he becomes a clinging clot for a similar period, then a lump of flesh for a similar period. Then an angel is sent to him and instructed concerning four matters. He is told: ‘Write down his provision, his lifespan, his deeds, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.’ Then the spirit is breathed into him.”

He thus explained, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that after the fetus’s body is created in its mother’s womb, but before the spirit is breathed into it, its provision, lifespan, deeds, and whether it will be wretched or blessed are recorded. In the same way, the account of the foremost of Adam’s descendants was recorded while Adam still lay in his clay form, before the spirit was breathed into him.

As for the wording quoted by some people:

“I was a prophet while Adam was between water and clay,”

this report is invalid both in its transmission and in terms of reason. Adam was not between water and clay; rather, clay consists of water and earth. He was, however, between spirit and body. Thus, this and similar reports concern

---

## Page 9506

Allah’s knowledge of things before they come into being, His recording them, and His informing others about them. This is distinct from their actual existence, for they do not actually exist until they are created. Whoever fails to distinguish between a thing’s being established in knowledge, speech, and writing and its reality in the external world—or between existence in knowledge and actual existence—is profoundly ignorant and astray.

Scholars have condemned in the strongest terms those who say that a nonexistent thing is an entity established in the external world. Although these people have a specious rational argument, supposing that its distinctness in knowledge and will entails its distinctness in the external world, they are mistaken. The correct understanding is to distinguish between being established in knowledge and being established in external reality. As for the claim that things exist before they are created, this is still greater ignorance and misguidance.

As for his claim that all the saints, even the prophets, derive knowledge from the Seal of the Saints, this contradicts both reason and revelation. The prophets are superior to the saints, and the best saints are those who follow the prophets most closely. Thus, Abū Bakr was the best person upon whom the sun has risen after the prophets and messengers.

Likewise, his claim that the Seal of the Saints derives outward knowledge from the same source as the Prophet, but derives inward knowledge from the source from which the angel receives what he reveals to the Prophet, is among the gravest forms of unbelief and misguidance. It rests upon the doctrine of the proponents of philosophy, who regard prophethood as an emanation flowing into the prophet’s intellect and say that the angel

---

## Page 9507

is what takes shape within the prophet’s soul as luminous forms. Thus they say that the prophet receives knowledge from those images of the imagination, which, in their view, constitute the angel. Whoever receives intelligible meanings from the immaterial intellect is therefore greater and more perfect than someone who receives them from imagined representations. These people adopted the doctrines of those heretical philosophers and followed the path of spiritual discipline. They then began speaking of those heretical philosophical matters, presenting them in the guise of unveilings and communications.

What they have said about the Seal of the Saints has no basis in reality. Although al-Hakīm al-Tirmidhī mentioned it in his book Khātam al-Awliyā’ (The Seal of the Saints), he committed an error in that book that is well known to people of understanding, knowledge, and faith. These matters are discussed at length elsewhere.

No one may narrate or relate these hadiths and similar reports, which scholars recognize as lies and fabrications—especially when their falsity is established by reason; indeed, they are mere figments of the mind—except to explain that they are false. As authentically reported in the Sahīh, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever narrates a hadith from me while believing it to be a lie is one of the liars.”

Those in authority must prevent these reports from being related anywhere. Whoever persists in doing so must receive a severe punishment that deters him and others like him from lying about the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, his Companions, his household, and other people of knowledge and religion. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 9508

He also said—may Allah have mercy on him:

In the two Sahīhs, Abū Mūsā reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Every Muslim must give charity.”
He was asked, “What if he has nothing to give?”
He replied, “He should work with his own hands, benefiting himself and giving charity.”
He was asked, “What if he is unable to do so?”
He replied, “He should help someone in need and distress.”
He was asked, “What if he is unable to do so?”
He replied, “He should enjoin what is right or good.”
He was asked, “What if he does not do that?”
He replied, “He should refrain from evil, for that is charity.”

The two Sahīhs also report that Abū Dharr said:

“I asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, which deeds are best?’
He replied, ‘Faith in Allah and striving in His cause.’
I asked, ‘Which slaves are best to free?’
He replied, ‘Those most prized by their owners and highest in price.’
I asked, ‘What if I do not do that?’
He replied, ‘Help a craftsman, or do work for someone who lacks the skill.’
I asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, what if I am too weak to perform some of these deeds?’
He replied, ‘Refrain from harming people, for that is charity you give on your own behalf.’”

In this hadith, he made charity obligatory upon every Muslim and set out five levels as alternatives to one another. The first is to give charity from one’s wealth; if one has none, one should earn money

---

## Page 9509

and thereby benefit himself and give charity. This is evidence that earning a living is obligatory. If he is unable to do so, he should help someone in need through physical effort; if he cannot, then through his speech. If he does not do that, he should refrain from evil. The first two involve wealth, whether already possessed or newly earned; the next two involve bodily effort, whether with one’s hands or one’s tongue.

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Dharr reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Each morning, charity is due for every joint in each of you. Every saying of ‘Glory be to Allah’ is charity; every saying of ‘Praise be to Allah’ is charity; every saying of ‘There is no god but Allah’ is charity; and every saying of ‘Allah is the Greatest’ is charity. Enjoining good is charity, and forbidding evil is charity. Two units of prayer performed in the forenoon suffice for all of that.”

In this hadith, he counted the four utterances, enjoining good, and forbidding evil as charity. Two units of the forenoon prayer suffice.

It also reports from him:

“Some of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ‘Messenger of Allah, the wealthy have taken all the rewards. They pray as we pray and fast as we fast, but they also give charity from their surplus wealth.’

“He replied, ‘Has Allah not given you something with which to give charity? Every saying of “Glory be to Allah” is charity; every saying of “Allah is the Greatest” is charity; every saying of “Praise be to Allah” is charity; and every saying of “There is no god but Allah” is charity. Enjoining good is charity, and forbidding evil is charity. And when any of you has sexual intercourse, that too is charity.’

“They said, ‘Messenger of Allah, can one of us satisfy his desire and be rewarded for it?’

“He replied:

---

## Page 9510

“‘Tell me, if he were to satisfy his desire unlawfully, would he incur sin? Likewise, if he satisfies it lawfully, he will be rewarded.’”

I say: It seems—and Allah knows best—that his description of it as “charity” means that it takes the place of the charity given by the wealthy. The second hadith would thus explain the first, unlike the hadith of Abū Mūsā, which makes charity obligatory. Alternatively, it may be charity that a person gives to himself, as in the earlier hadith of Abū Dharr: “You refrain from harming people.”

---

## Page 9511

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was also asked about hadiths attributed to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, which storytellers and others relate in the streets and elsewhere.

He gave the following answers:

Among these is their report that he said:

“My Lord has taught me good manners, and has taught me well.”

He replied: “Praise be to Allah. The meaning is sound, but no reliable chain of transmission is known for it.”

They also report that he, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“If the believer were on a mountain peak, Allah would appoint someone to harm him, or a devil to harm him.”

He replied: “Praise be to Allah. This is not known to be among the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

They also report that he, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“If this world were fresh blood, the believer’s sustenance from it would be lawful.”

He replied: “Praise be to Allah. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, nor is this known to have been transmitted from him through any chain of transmission. However, Allah will certainly make available to the believer sufficient provision to meet his needs. It is impossible under Islamic law for a believer to be forbidden what he cannot do without, for Allah

---

## Page 9512

has not imposed upon the believers what they cannot do, nor forbidden them what necessity compels them to use through no sin of their own.” This was stated and written by Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah.

Among the reports they also attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, as relating from Allah, is:

“Neither My heaven nor My earth can contain Me, but the heart of My believing servant can.”

He replied: “Praise be to Allah. This appears among the reports transmitted from the Children of Israel. No known chain of transmission traces it back to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. The meaning of ‘His heart contains Me’ is faith in Me, love for Me, and knowledge of Me. It does not mean, as some say, that Allah’s essence dwells in people’s hearts. This is a belief of the Christians, who restricted it to Christ alone.”

Among the reports they also attribute to him is:

“The heart is the house of the Lord.”

He replied: “Praise be to Allah. This statement is of the same kind as the preceding one: the heart is the home of faith in Allah, knowledge of Him, and love for Him. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they also attribute to him is:

“I was an unknown treasure, and I wished to be known. So I created creatures and made Myself known to them, and they knew Me.”

He replied: “These are not words spoken by Allah to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. No chain of transmission is known for this report, whether sound or weak.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, said:

“When the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, spoke with Abū Bakr, I was like a black African between them”—someone who does not understand.

---

## Page 9513

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is an obvious lie. No hadith scholar has transmitted it; only an ignorant person or a heretic would relate it.”

Among the reports they attribute to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“I am the city of knowledge, and ‘Alī is its gate.”

He replied:

“This hadith is weak—indeed, fabricated according to hadith experts. Al-Tirmidhī and others have transmitted it, but despite this, it is a lie.”

Among the reports they attribute to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Allah will offer an apology to the poor on the Day of Resurrection, saying: ‘By My might and majesty, worldly things were not withheld from you because you were of little worth to Me. Rather, I wished to raise your standing on this Day. Go to the gathering place, and whoever showed you kindness with a morsel of bread, gave you a drink of water, or clothed you with a piece of cloth, take them with you to Paradise.’”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This account is a lie. No hadith scholar has transmitted it. It is false and, by consensus, contrary to the Quran and Sunnah.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“When he arrived in Medina during the Hijrah, the girls of Banū al-Najjār came out with frame drums, singing:

‘The full moon has risen upon us …
From the passes of al-Wadā‘’

—and so on to the end of the poem. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said: ‘Shake your karābīl; may Allah bless you!’”

He replied:

“As for women beating frame drums at weddings, this was a known practice in the time of

---

## Page 9514

the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him. As for the words attributed to him, ‘Shake your karābīl; may Allah bless you!’ these are not known to have been reported from him, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him is that he said:

“If Abū Bakr’s faith were weighed against the faith of all people, Abū Bakr’s faith would outweigh it.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. The meaning of this is conveyed in a well-known hadith in the Sunan collections: Abū Bakr, may Allah be pleased with him, was weighed against this community and outweighed it.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“O Allah, You have brought me out of the place most beloved to me, so settle me in the place most beloved to You.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is false. Rather, it is established in al-Tirmidhī and elsewhere that he addressed Mecca, saying: ‘By Allah, you are the most beloved of Allah’s lands to Allah.’ He also said: ‘You are the most beloved of lands to me.’ Thus, he stated that it was the most beloved of lands both to Allah and to himself.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Whoever visits me and my forefather Abraham in the same year will enter Paradise.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This hadith is a lie and a fabrication. No hadith scholar has transmitted it.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Your poor.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording has not been transmitted, but its meaning is sound: the poor are recipients of kindness, and through them good deeds are earned.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Blessing is with your elders.”

---

## Page 9515

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. It is established in the Sahīh, in a hadith narrated by Jubayr, that he said: ‘Let the elder speak; let the elder speak.’ That is, the older person should speak. It is also established in the hadith concerning leadership in prayer that he said: ‘If they are equal—in Quran recitation, knowledge of the Sunnah, and emigration—then the oldest of them should lead them in prayer.’”

Among the reports they also relate is:

“An elder among his people is like a prophet among his community.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him; rather, this is something some people say.”

Among the reports they also relate is:

“If a believer’s fear and hope were weighed, they would balance each other.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is reported from some of the early generations, and its meaning is sound.”

Among the reports they relate about ʿAlī, may Allah be pleased with him, is that a Bedouin rushed through his prayer. ʿAlī told him, “Do not rush through your prayer.” The Bedouin replied, “Had your father prayed that way, he would not have entered the Fire.”

---

## Page 9516

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is a lie. They have also related it about ‘Umar, but that too is a lie.”

Among the reports they relate about ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, is that he killed his father.

He replied:

“This is a lie. ‘Umar’s father died in the pre-Islamic era, before the Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, was sent.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“I was a prophet when Adam was between water and clay, and I was a prophet when Adam was neither water nor clay.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is false and baseless. Rather, the transmitted wording, related by al-Tirmidhī and others, is that he was asked: ‘O Messenger of Allah, when were you a prophet?’ He replied: ‘When Adam was between spirit and body.’ In the Sunan, it is narrated from al-‘Irbād ibn Sāriyah that he said: ‘I was indeed recorded with Allah as the Seal of the Prophets while Adam still lay in his clay.’”

Among the reports they also relate is:

“The unmarried man’s bed is made of fire. Pitiable is a man without a wife, and pitiable is a woman without a husband.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. I have not found this narrated, nor has it been established as authentic.”

Among the reports they relate is:

“When Abraham, peace be upon him, built the House, he prayed a thousand units of prayer at each corner. Allah Most High then revealed to him: ‘O Abraham, better than this is relieving someone’s hunger or covering someone’s nakedness.’”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is an obvious lie. It is not found in any of the books of the Muslims.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“When Abraham and I are mentioned, invoke blessings upon him, then invoke blessings upon me. When I and the other prophets besides him are mentioned, invoke blessings upon me, then invoke blessings upon them.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is not known in the books of the scholars, nor is it known from any scholar recognized for his knowledge of hadith.”

---

## Page 9517

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Whoever eats with someone who has been forgiven will be forgiven.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. The scholars have transmitted no chain of narration for this, nor is it found in any of the books of the Muslims. They merely relate it from Sālim. Its meaning is not true without qualification, for disbelievers and hypocrites may eat with Muslims.”

Among the reports they also relate is:

“Whoever relieves someone’s hunger or covers someone’s nakedness, I guarantee him Paradise.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is not known to have come from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they relate is:

“Do not hate tribulations, for through them the hypocrites are cut down.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is not known to have come from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they relate is:

“Reviling my Companions is a sin that will not be forgiven.”

He—may Allah have mercy on him—replied:

“This is a lie attributed to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. Allah Most High has said:

‘Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.’”

Among the reports they relate is:

“Whoever teaches his brother a verse from the Book of Allah has thereby gained ownership of him as a slave.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is a lie. It is not found in any of the books of the scholars.”

Among the reports they attribute to him is:

“A verse of the Quran is better than Muhammad and his family.”

---

## Page 9518

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. The Quran is the speech of Allah, revealed and uncreated, so it is not to be likened to created beings. The wording cited has not been transmitted.”

Among the reports they attribute to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“I am of the Arabs, but the Arabs are not of me.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they also attribute to him is:

“O Allah, let me live poor, cause me to die poor, and gather me among the poor.”

He replied:

“This is reported, but it is weak and has not been reliably established. Its meaning is: ‘Let me live submissive and humble.’ However, the wording has not been reliably established.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“When you hear a hadith attributed to me, compare it with the Book and the Sunnah. If it agrees with them, relate it; if it does not, do not.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is reported, but it is weak according to several imams, including al-Shāfiʿī and others.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“O Alī, get yourself a pair of iron sandals and wear them out in seeking knowledge, even in China.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. Neither this nor that is among the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

---

## Page 9519

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘Meet Me with your intentions, and do not meet Me with your deeds.’”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is not known to have been reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“Whoever brings a jug to someone performing ablution is as though he had provided a saddled and bridled steed to be ridden in battle in Allah’s cause.”

He replied:

“These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, nor is this report known in any of the recognized books of the Muslims.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“A time will come upon my community when no one will preserve his religion except someone who flees from one mountain peak to another.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is not known to have been reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“The good deeds of the righteous are sins for those brought near to Allah.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. These are the words of certain people, not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“They concealed a truce from my Companions: the killer and the one killed are in Paradise.”

---

## Page 9520

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is not known to have been reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him is:

“When discussion turns to the disputes among my Companions, refrain; and when discussion turns to divine decree and predestination, refrain.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This has been transmitted through a disconnected chain, and it has no established chain of transmission.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“When tribulations become widespread, seek refuge in the outlying regions of Yemen.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This wording is not known.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“Whoever spends the night guarded by a dog spends the night under the Lord’s wrath.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. These are not the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is:

“He instructed women to show amorous playfulness toward their husbands during intercourse.”

He replied:

“This does not come from him, peace and blessings be upon him.”

Among the reports they attribute to him, peace and blessings be upon him, is that he said:

“Whoever breaks a heart must mend it.”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. This is a matter of good conduct. This wording is not known to have been reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, and much of what people say may be true

---

## Page 9521

but it may be attributed to the Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, so long as it is not objectionable. For this wording does not apply without qualification to breaking the hearts of disbelievers and hypocrites, since doing so serves to uphold the religion. And Allah knows best. May Allah bestow abundant blessings and peace upon our master Muhammad, his family and his Companions until the Day of Judgment, and upon his family, his Companions, his wives and the Successors.”
